# Kernel-ideas sweep 2026-09-26 — LEDGER

Branch `worktree-kernel-ideas-2026-09-26` (base 361d4f9 = production). Nothing here is merged; candidates live under `scratch/kernel_ideas/<family>/`, reviewer artefacts under `<family>/review/`. Numbers are kernel-level medians against the UNMODIFIED production code object built with the production flags, at production shapes; "reviewer x" is the independent re-run. est columns are engineer estimates (kernel delta x production call count), not e2e measurements.

Totals: 81 ideas, 34 measured wins, 28 CONFIRMED by review, 2 REFUTED.

#### `E_indexer/score_qreg_hw` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `indexer_score_wmma_batched_mw_e2m1`
- idea: Hold the wave's 16 Q A-fragments (64 VGPRs) and its 16 head weights in registers instead of re-reading them from LDS for every 16-key n-tile, plus a one-tile register prefetch of the 9 packed key words (s2_w8n8_pf_hw in cand_score2.hip). Same WMMA k-tile order, relu*hw sum order and shfl_xor(16) reduce -> bit-exact. Confirmed medians-of-3 (cold keys): 235K b4 275.8 -> 139.5 us (0.506x, 88% of the 123 us DRAM floor); 235K b1 82.9 -> 42.5; 131K b1/b4 47.4/157.7 -> 26.5/83.1; 65K b1/b4 32.8/84.8 -> 20.8/45.1. Arithmetic: 16 index-layer calls per step (8 layers x 2 lanes) x savings 39.8/74.6/136.3 us = 0.64 / 1.19 / 2.18 ms per step at 65K/131K/235K (b=4 per lane). Prefill uses the gemm variant: 0%.
- engineer: 275.76 -> 139.48 us, x1.977, runs=4
- correctness: bit_exact=True; bit-exact vs production kernel on every row of every shape (kb::compare_f32 bit_diff=0 at n=65536/131072/235000, b=1/4, incl. the -inf tail stamps); 139 VGPRs, no spills
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=2.18, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=drop-in
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer/cand_score2.hip (s2_w8n8_pf_hw), cand_score.hip (score_mw_pf_qreg, first version), results/score_cand2.txt, results/score_confirm_run{1,2,3}.txt, harness.cpp, NOTES.md
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer && bash build.sh && bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 156 --label E_indexer/confirm -- bash run_confirm.sh confirm 3 score && python3 summarize.py results/score_confirm_run{1,2,3}.txt`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.974 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Reproduced. My 3 separate-process runs (scheduler label review/E_indexer, hub down, rounds=50) at 235K b=4 cold keys: base 275.44 us (min/max 275.32/275.60), cand s2_w8n8_pf_hw 139.56 us (139.40/139.60) = 1.974x vs claimed 1.977x; every other shape within 0.5-2% of the engineer's table (65K b1 32.96->21.12, 65K b4 85.24->44.24, 131K b1 47.68->26.84, 131K b4 157.52->83.20, 235K b1 82.96->42.52). Files: scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer/review/results/score_confirm_run{1,2,3}.txt, review_extra.txt. Baseline fidelity: my base hsaco built from the unmodified in-tree indexer_score_wmma.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco is byte-identical to the engineer's; harness launches the production symbol indexer_score_wmma_batched_mw_e2m1 with grid (ceil(n_idx_max/1024), b) x 256 and the exact argument order of IndexerScoreWmma::launch_batched_mw_e2m1_rows (scores,q,hw,keys,n_idx_per,n_idx_stride,keys_base_per), keys_base_per non-null as the decode arena passes it (forward_prefill.rs:3341/5489-5500); candidate has the identical signature/grid and includes the same e2m1_key_common.inc. Cold-key regime matches production (keys streamed once per layer/lane/step); the harness times direct launches while production is graph-captured, so I added graph inner=1 + flush: ratio 0.50-0.52 at b=8/235K, b=3/100003, b=1/368640, i.e. the win is not a launch artifact. Warm graph numbers show the same ~0.4-0.47 ratio, consistent with the LDS-bound diagnosis. Correctness: bit-exact (bit_diff=0) on all 12 engineer shapes in all 3 runs and on 28 additional cases I ran (2 input distributions incl. negative head weights; n=17, 1000, 1023, 1025, 4097, 100003, 235001, 368640=max ctx; b=3 and b=8; per-row DIFFERENT n_comp including rows with n_comp=0 and 1 plus shuffled keys_base_per; null keys_base_per), with a full-buffer sentinel compare showing identical -inf stamp counts and identical untouched regions, so no skipped or extra writes. No work skipped: prefetch loads are guarded by c_row < n_comp, tails stamped identically, same WMMA k-order/relu*hw order/shfl_xor reduce. Minor observations: (1) the engineer's on-disk cand_score2 hsaco differs in bytes from a fresh build of the on-disk cand_score2.hip (harness also references s3_* kernels absent from the source), but the s2_w8n8_pf_hw disassembly is identical line-for-line (1453 lines, 0 diff) and all symbols/VGPR counts match, so the measured kernel is the one in the source; (2) 139 VGPRs vs 59 lowers occupancy but the kernel now sits at 88-92% of the 640 GB/s DRAM floor for b>=3, so that is moot; (3) est_ms_per_token 2.18 ms is arithmetically consistent (16 calls x 136 us) but is a projection under the stated premise (8 index layers x 2 lanes, b=4 per lane, all rows at 235K, indexer on the decode critical path); it is not an end-to-end measurement and only applies when n_comp > 512 on the index layers.
  - merge notes: Drop-in body replacement of indexer_score_wmma_batched_mw_e2m1 in crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/indexer_score_wmma.hip (the s2_w8n8_pf_hw instantiation with WAVES=8, NT=8, PF=true, HWREG=true, VSTAGE=false); no Rust wrapper change (same signature, grid, block, LDS). New implicit requirements to document/assert: head_weights row (head_weights + bi*32 floats) must be 16-B aligned because HWREG uses float4 global loads (true today: sd.indexer_head_weights is a dedicated hipMalloc DeviceBuffer of b*32 floats, but a future suballocation with an unaligned offset would break it, so add a debug_assert or fall back to scalar loads); lds_q needs the aligned(16) attribute the candidate adds (16-B ds loads of the A-fragments). Since the change is bit-exact, the existing indexer_score_mw_oracle / indexer_score_e2m1 / indexer_pipeline tests should pass unchanged; run them plus multistream_row_bases (per-row keys_base) and bench_indexer_score_isolated at 65K/131K/235K b=1,4,8. The prefill gemm variant (indexer_score_wmma_gemm_e2m1) is untouched; only the decode arena path benefits. Re-measure the decode step end-to-end (bench_decode_latency_breakdown at 131K/235K) after integration to confirm the projected 1.2-2.2 ms/step actually lands on the critical path.

#### `E_indexer/topk_select_hybrid_sort_vec4` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `indexer_topk_select_batched_ilp`
- idea: Keep the exact sample/threshold/compact/tie logic; run both bitonic sorts as hybrid networks (stages with j<32 pair lanes of one wave -> __shfl_xor on registers, no LDS, no barrier; j>=32 through LDS, double-buffered for the 2048-key sort): barriers per row 144 -> 56. Count/compact passes read float4 (4x bytes in flight per loadcnt slot, U=8 chunks). Selection is identical (same comparator/network, integer counts, total order). Confirmed medians-of-3, select alone b=4: 65K 60.1 -> 38.3, 131K 67.6 -> 43.4, 235K 85.4 -> 58.8 us (0.57-0.63x; b=1 same). Arithmetic: 16 calls/step x 21.8/24.2/26.6 us = 0.35/0.39/0.43 ms per step at 65K/131K/235K. Prefill: 1568 calls of 512 WGs at 100K, ~0.6x -> ~0.2-0.3%.
- engineer: 85.42 -> 58.80 us, x1.453, runs=4
- correctness: bit_exact=True; selected[] and done[] identical to the production select+chain on real scores, tie-heavy (quantised to 0.25) scores and tie+candidate-mask (-inf 3/4) scores at n=65536/131072/235000, b=1/4 (SEL exact=YES, 54 checks per run); 94 VGPRs, no spills

Odd shapes I added (n=4097 / 100003 / 235001, b=1 and b=8): 1.45-1.76x, all exact. Baseline is faithful: base_indexer_topk_bitonic hsaco rebuilt by me from the unmodified in-tree source with $KFLAGS_V41 is byte-identical to the engineer's; same symbol (indexer_topk_select_batched_ilp = production default V41_TOPK_SELECT_ILP on), same grid (b)x1024, same args/stride 369152/top_k 512 as launch_batched_sel, same regime (scores from the production score kernel, warm in L2, graph-captured) as decode. Candidate hsaco rebuilt from cand_topk.hip is ISA-identical (0 diff lines). 94 VGPR, 0 spills, wave32 (the __shfl_xor j<32 stages need wave32; base kernel already assumes it). Correctness: 54/54 harness checks exact in each run; my extra harness (review/extra.cpp -> review/results/extra_shapes.txt) is exact vs production for ragged n per row {235000,234999,4096,4097,511,1,32768,32769}, float4-chunk edges {32767,32771,36863,36865,8191,8193,4095,100003}, an n=0 row, b=8, all-equal, -inf-dominated, and a constructed give-up trap (all 4 rows done=0 on both kernels, chain result identical and equal to a CPU total-order sort). The two FAIL lines in extra_shapes.txt are my CPU reference not modelling production's '-inf positions come back as -1' semantic; base and candidate agree on them (sel_diff=0). Caveats, none refuting: (1) the '0.57-0.63x' text in the claim compares candidate-ALONE to the base select+CHAIN number; alone-vs-alone is 0.64-0.69x and the production stage (select+early-out chain, which the candidate still needs) goes 92.6 -> ~66 us at 235K b4 = ~1.40x; the absolute saving (21.8/24.2/26.6 us per call) and the speedup field 1.453 are correct. (2) est_ms_per_token 0.43 is per STEP (16 calls x 26.6 us, 2 lanes x 4 rows = 8 tokens), i.e. ~0.05 ms per token; small versus step time. (3) NaN scores make the candidate's register-phase comparator drop/duplicate elements (1871 diffs in my NaN sprinkle) whereas production only misorders; production output is garbage with NaN anyway, but note it. (4) float4 loads assume the scores row base is 16-B aligned: n_idx_stride % 4 == 0 (369152 ok; the oracle test's strides 9000/24576/49152 ok) and a hipMalloc'd base with no sub-offset; no runtime check in the kernel.

Add debug_assert!(n_idx_stride % 4 == 0) (or a static assert on ATTN_MIXED_MAX_KEYS) and keep passing the whole scores buffer (16-B aligned) in launch_batched_sel; consider a scalar fallback if stride%4 != 0. Keep indexer_topk_select_batched (U=1) as the V41_TOPK_SELECT_ILP=0 rollback. Run tests/indexer_topk_select_oracle.rs (indexer_topk_select_matches_chain, ilp_matches) plus bench_indexer_topk_isolated / bench_decode_latency_ab before/after; add a give-up-path case (>4096-element distinct cluster at un-sampled positions) since the existing cases never hit done=0. Wave32 assumption (__shfl_xor with j<32) must hold: gfx1201 code object is wave32, same as the existing __shfl_down reduction. Expected production effect: -21.8/-24.2/-26.6 us per select call at 65K/131K/235K, ~0.35-0.43 ms per decode step (16 calls), ~0.2-0.3% of 100K prefill; not an ms-per-token-scale win, but bit-exact and cheap. No KLD gate needed (selection identical).

#### `E_indexer/gather_b128` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `indexer_gather_batched`
- idea: One b128 (16 B) per thread instead of one f16 (2 B): 64 threads per 1 KB row, grid (top_k, b) x 64 (gather_u4_r1); pure copy, sentinel rows skipped as production. Confirmed medians-of-3, production decode regime (cold store, one graph-launched kernel, random picks): b=4 25.0 -> 18.6 us (0.74x), b=1 17.0 -> 15.7; direct cold b=4 19.7 -> 13.4, b=32 110 -> 50; prefill lane shape b=512 LOCAL picks 1417 -> 522 us (0.37x, 1.03 TB/s effective). Arithmetic decode: 38 layers (8 index + 30 reuse) x 2 lanes = 76 calls/step x 6.5 us = 0.49 ms per step at any n. Prefill at 100K: reuse-gather stage 4.05 s = 5880 calls x 689 us; at 0.4-0.45x (synthetic locality is worse than production's) saves ~2.2-2.5 s, plus <=0.65 s of index-layer gathers in prefill_indexer -> ~1.8% of 160 s.
- engineer: 25.04 -> 18.56 us, x1.349, runs=4
- correctness: bit_exact=True; destination bit-identical to production for random and local selections at b=1/4/32/512 (CMP bitexact=YES, 18 checks per run); head_dim must satisfy head_dim*2 % 16 == 0 (512 does)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.49, est_prefill_pct=1.80; integration_cost=small
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer/cand_gather.hip (gather_u4_r1; u4_r4 equivalent with 256-thread blocks), results/gather_cand2.txt, results/gather_b512.txt, results/gather_confirm_run{1,2,3}.txt
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer && bash build.sh && bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 156 --label E_indexer/confirm -- bash run_confirm.sh confirm 3 gather && python3 summarize.py results/gather_confirm_run{1,2,3}.txt; b=512: bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 720 --label E_indexer/b512 -- env GATHER_B512=1 GATHER_CANDS=gather_u4_r1,gather_u4_r4,gather_u4_r4_2ipt bash run_rest.sh `
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.351 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Reproduced and correct, but with one material hole the engineer did not test. (1) Baseline fidelity: I rebuilt base_indexer_gather from the unmodified in-tree K/indexer_gather.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco (byte-identical to the engineer's hsaco) and the candidate from cand_gather.hip (ISA identical to the engineer's object, only the embedded path differs). The harness launches `indexer_gather_batched` with grid (top_k=512, b, 2) x 256 and args (dst, comp_kv, selected, 512, head_dim=512, comp_base_per) exactly as S/indexer.rs:1151 launch_batched_rows does from FP:5653/5728; the production main store IS F16 (docs/v41/AUDIT_2026-09-22.md:318: the COMP_KV_FP8 branch needs ratio==4, which v41 has no layer for), buffers are whole hipMalloc allocations (batch_scratch.rs:1407 mk_u16 = DeviceBuffer::new; state.rs:217), so the candidate's 16-B alignment assumption holds. Cold-store + one graph-launched kernel matches decode. (2) Repro, 3 separate processes via the scheduler (review/results/gather_review_run{1,2,3}.txt): b=4 graph cold random 24.80 -> 18.36 us (u4_r1), ratio 0.740 = 1.351x vs claimed 1.349x; b=1 16.24 -> 14.96 (0.92); direct cold b=4 19.52 -> 13.40, b=32 109.9 -> 50.4; b=512 LOCAL cold 1408 -> 521 us (0.370). All within 1-2% of the claim. Extra: b=8 40.8 -> 24.4 (0.60). (3) Correctness: engineer's 18 CMP checks pass; my independent checker (review/gather_b128/gcheck.cpp, results/gcheck.txt) adds 33 checks the engineer lacked: scattered and trailing sentinel rows (destination pre-filled and verified untouched), comp_base_per == nullptr (prefill path) and per-row bases (arena), odd b=7 / odd top_k=333, top_k=1, all-sentinel rows, b=5, b=8, b=512, b=1024 (max prefill chunk), an odd 100003-row store with arbitrary 16-bit patterns (NaN/Inf/denormal encodings) - all bit-exact vs production, and production vs CPU reference. Pure copy, no precision change, no KLD gate needed. (4) THE HOLE: at b=2 the candidate is consistently SLOWER: engineer's harness (different picks) random cold direct 12.7 -> 21.1 us (1.66x), graph 18.3 -> 28.1 (1.53x), local graph 19.7 -> 28.8 (1.46x), warm 8.2 -> 17.9 (2.2x); my 235K-row probe (review/gather_b128/gtime_run{1,2}.txt) 13.6 -> 21.2 direct, 18.2 -> 27.8 graph, tight min/max over 2 runs. Both u4_r1 and u4_r4 show it; u4_r4_2ipt does not (0.74-0.81x at b=2) but that variant loses at b=4 cold (1.13-1.19x). b=3 (0.77), b=5 (0.70), b=6 (0.68) win. The same pathology appears in the engineer's own warm inner=5 data at b=4 (1.63x slower, dismissed as 'not the production regime'). Root cause not chased (looks like an even-b write-stream aliasing or dispatch effect). Production decode runs ONE lane below 6 rows with b = 1-5 per lane (INVENTORY:30-31), so b=2 is a real production shape. (5) est_ms_per_token arithmetic: '76 calls x 6.5 us = 0.49 ms at any n' assumes 2 lanes x b=4 = 8 rows. At 1-5 rows there are 38 calls/step: saving is 0.05 ms (b=1), 0.18 (b=3), 0.24 (b=4), 0.29 (b=5), and a LOSS of ~0.37 ms at b=2. Even the best case is ~0.15% of a ~300 ms 8-row step. (6) Prefill: the synthetic b=512 baseline (1.2-1.6 ms/call) is 2x slower than the production stage's 689 us/call, so production picks are far more cache-local than the engineer's generator; my locality sweep (1.5K/8-token, 4K, 16K, 64K-row windows, cold and warm) gives ratios 0.32-0.44 throughout, so the ~0.4 discount and ~1.5-2% of 160 s are plausible but remain an extrapolation, not a measurement.
  - merge notes: Integration must NOT be a blind drop-in. (a) Change S/indexer.rs:1151 launch_batched_rows grid to (top_k, batch) x 64 for gather_u4_r1 and keep head_dim*2 % 16 == 0 as a debug_assert (512 satisfies it; the kernel divides by tpr at runtime). (b) Gate by batch: at batch == 2 (and verify b=6 warm, which was ~1.0-1.15x) dispatch the production kernel (or gather_u4_r4_2ipt, which wins at b=2 but loses at b=4), or first find and fix the even-b pathology; re-measure b=1..8 graph+cold after the fix, 3 processes each, and include b=2 in the confirm set. (c) Keep the null comp_base_per path (prefill) - verified bit-exact here. (d) Bit-exact copy: no fidelity gate needed, but run the existing indexer/gather unit tests and one prefill_100k stage timing to confirm the k.indexer.gather / _reuse stage numbers (expect ~0.4x of 4.05 s, i.e. ~2.4 s saved) since the prefill figure is extrapolated from synthetic locality. (e) The decode win is small in absolute terms (0.05-0.3 ms/step depending on rows); prioritise below the score/select candidates in this family. #### `E_indexer/candidate_threshold_ilp` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `candidate_threshold`
- idea: Same 4-pass 8-bit radix select, but block 1024 (launch knob) with 8 independent loads per thread per pass (candidate_threshold_ilp in cand_cand.hip). Threshold is the exact topk-th largest key -> identical. Confirmed medians-of-3: 65K 41.9 -> 30.0, 131K 57.3 -> 32.4, 235K 87.4 -> 43.8 us (b=1 and b=4 the same, 1 WG per row). Runs once per lane-step (L20): 2 x 11.9/25/43.6 us = 0.02/0.05/0.09 ms per step. Still a single WG with a serial bin scan: a parallel scan or 16-bit passes could halve it again (not done).
- engineer: 87.36 -> 43.82 us, x1.994, runs=4
- correctness: bit_exact=True; threshold[b] bit-identical to production at all shapes (CMP bitexact=YES); 29 VGPRs
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.09, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=small
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer/cand_cand.hip, results/cand_cand2.txt, results/cand_confirm_run{1,2,3}.txt
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer && bash build.sh && bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 156 --label E_indexer/confirm -- bash run_confirm.sh confirm 3 cand && python3 summarize.py results/cand_confirm_run{1,2,3}.txt`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.998 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Baseline fidelity: in-tree crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/candidate_blocks.hip and src/candidate_blocks.rs are byte-identical to 361d4f9; my fresh $KFLAGS_V41 --genco build of the baseline is byte-identical to the engineer's base_candidate_blocks_gfx1201.hsaco; the harness launches the production symbol `candidate_threshold` with the production geometry/args (grid (b), block 256; threshold, block_score, n_per, nb_stride=ceil(369152/8)=46144, cb_size=8, topk=2048), block_score produced by the production candidate_block_max from the production score kernel, warm-L2 block scores (matches production: block_max writes them just before). One regime nit: the harness times graph inner=5 but the production call (FP:5594, de.compute) is a direct launch with no capture in that region; I re-timed DIRECT inner=1 and the delta is unchanged (235K b=1: 85.8 -> 35.4 us, 0.41x), so this does not flatter the candidate. Repro: engineer's exact harness section rebuilt by me under review/threshold/, 3 separate processes rounds=50: 235K b=1 87.46 -> 43.78 us (0.501x, 1.998x; claim 87.36 -> 43.82), 131K b=1 57.59 -> 32.49, 65K b=1 42.54 -> 30.54, b=4 same within 1% -- all within 0.5% of the claim. Correctness: bit-identical thresholds on 12654 + 2x33918 row checks vs production kernel AND vs a CPU nth_element reference, over n in {16384 (vacuous), 16385, 16392, 17000, 20000, 30000, 50000, 65536, 100003, 131072, 235000, 368640 (max ctx)}, b in {1,4,8,512,1024}, per-row DIFFERENT n_per, and 6 input distributions (score-like, tie-heavy quantised, all-equal, half -inf, 4-valued, 1e30 magnitudes). Exactness is structural (same histogram counts, same serial scan) so no KLD gate needed. ISA: 29 VGPR, 1040 B LDS, no spills, as claimed; the engineer's and my candidate code objects disassemble identically. The claim's decode arithmetic (2 calls/step at L20 -> 0.02/0.05/0.09 ms) is honest. THE REAL PROBLEM (untested by the engineer): the same wrapper launch_build() also serves PREFILL with b = tokens.len() = 512/1024 rows, and L20 has compress ratio 1 so n = token count (up to 100K+ at 100K prefill). At that shape the 1024-thread candidate is a stable regression across 3 process runs: n=20K b=512 82 -> 215 us (2.6x slower), 30K b=1024 174 -> 477 (2.7x), 65K b=512 117 -> 239 (2.0x), 65K b=1024 214 -> 454 (2.1x), 235K b=512 278 -> 318 (1.14x). Cause: the serial 256-bin scan by thread 0 idles 15 waves instead of 3 per WG in the throughput regime. est_prefill_pct=0 is therefore wrong in sign (absolute wall impact is small: roughly +20-25 ms per 100K prefill, ~0.015%, but it is a 2x kernel regression the notes never measured). A probe instantiation of the engineer's own templated body at BLOCK=256, U=8 (review/threshold/cand_review.hip, no new code) is faster than production at EVERY shape incl. prefill (65K b=512 0.85x, 235K b=512 0.72x, 235K b=4 0.53x) though slower than the 1024 variant at decode (235K b=4 48.6 vs 40.5 us). Second integration hazard: the engineer's note 'block 256 -> 1024 in S/candidate_blocks.rs:140' targets `const T` in launch_build, which is SHARED with the candidate_block_max launch; candidate_block_max has __launch_bounds__(256), so a naive T=1024 would make that launch fail.
  - merge notes: Merge as a body replacement of candidate_threshold with the templated cand_threshold_body and raise __launch_bounds__ to 1024 on that kernel only (keep candidate_block_max/mask_apply at 256). In S/candidate_blocks.rs launch_build (and launch_apply) give the threshold launch its own block size and gate it on batch: block 1024 when batch <= 8 (decode lanes: 0.38-0.71x), block 256 (with the same U=8 ILP body, i.e. the ilp256 instantiation) when batch is large (prefill chunks of 512/1024: 0.72-0.95x instead of 2.0-2.8x slower); or export both symbols and pick by batch. Do not touch the shared `const T`. Tests: candidate_blocks_oracle at n in {16384, 16385, 100003, 235000, 368640} with per-row different n_per and tie-heavy scores; add a prefill-shaped (b=512, n~65-100K) timing point to bench_decode_latency_breakdown or the family-E microbench so the geometry gate is exercised; re-check ISA for the 1024 variant on gfx1151 too if the iGPU code object is ever launched (candidate_blocks has a gfx1151 image). Expected decode gain is ~0.02-0.09 ms/step at 65K-235K (once per lane at L20), i.e. small; no fidelity gate needed (bit-identical thresholds). **Dead ends (E_indexer — dGPU (gfx1201) CSA2 sparse indexer + candidate pool: score mw_e2m1, top-k select, gather, candidate_block_max/threshold/mask_apply, idx-q matvec, indexer_fp4, vec_scale):** (1) Key-stream prefetch alone (score_mw_pf) and coalesced b128 key tiles via LDS (score_mw_coal): 1.01-1.07x SLOWER at every shape — the mw score kernel was LDS-bound on re-reading Q per n-tile (ATT: ~75% of stalls on ds_load_b128/s_wait_dscnt, 6% on s_wait_loadcnt), so anything that adds LDS traffic or only helps the key stream loses; once Q is in registers the coalesced tile ties but never wins. (2) Vectorised Q staging: +1-2% slower on top of pf_hw. (3) WG geometry: nothing beats 8 waves x 8 tiles; 2-4 waves x 32 tiles loses badly at b=1 (too few WGs). (4) Gather with 2-8 rows per thread: slower at b=4 cold (the production case) even after making the loads branch-free; the simple 1-row-per-thread b128 kernel is the pick. (5) The 'warm graph inner=5' gather regime inverts the ranking (base 12 us, candidates 20 us) because back-to-back launches rewrite the same 4 MB destination; it is not the production regime and was replaced by graph inner=1 + cold flush. (6) Gather b=4 LOCAL picks in the graph+cold mode is bimodal (17 vs 29 us) for every variant including base — not usable for claims. (7) ATT on the module-loaded select candidate produced empty CSVs (traces exist); not chased. (8) The 03:5x first-attempt numbers (hub live) had 2x p10/p90 spreads and were discarded in favour of the hub-down re-baseline.

**Notes (E_indexer — dGPU (gfx1201) CSA2 sparse indexer + candidate pool: score mw_e2m1, top-k select, gather, candidate_block_max/threshold/mask_apply, idx-q matvec, indexer_fp4, vec_scale):** All numbers are hub-DOWN (both GPUs idle), gfx1201 device 0 under the scheduler, kb::ab interleaved A/B, rounds 30 (exploration) / 50 x 3 separate processes (confirmation); medians-of-3 quoted, min/max within 1-3% except where flagged. Baselines are the unmodified in-tree kernels compiled with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco and launched with the production grid/args (S/indexer.rs, S/candidate_blocks.rs), scores stride 369152, per-row key bases (each decode row its own store), keys E2M1 80 B/row so the 235K store is 18.8 MB per row (the task text's 481 MB multiplied by heads; the real shapes were tested, no extrapolation needed). Regimes: score keys COLD (64 MB flush before each call, direct launch; the warm/graph numbers are also in the files and show the same ratios), select/candidate-pool scores WARM in L2 (they are written by the score kernel immediately before), gather store COLD with one graph-launched kernel (production decode) plus direct-launch cold and a b=512 LOCAL prefill point. Family total at b=4 per lane, 2 lanes: ~1.5 / 2.1 / 3.2 ms per decode step at 65K / 131K / 235K (score 0.64/1.19/2.18 + select 0.35/0.39/0.43 + gather 0.49 + threshold 0.02/0.05/0.09), all bit-exact / selection-identical, i.e. no fidelity gate needed. Prefill: ~1.8% from the gather (reuse-gather stage), ~0.25% from select; the score GEMM (68% of matrix peak) was not touched. Remaining headroom: score at b=1 is 42.5 us vs a 31 us floor (WG prologue + 230 WGs), select still ~40-59 us of pure latency (ideas 5/6/7), candidate_threshold still a 1-WG radix select (44 us). Everything reproducible from /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/E_indexer/{build.sh,build_cand.sh,run_score.sh,run_rest.sh,run_confirm.sh,summarize.py}; NOTES.md carries the full log, ISA stats, ATT summaries and the ranked idea list. No GPU processes were left running.

#### `F_mhc_glue/gemm_narrow_m24` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `f16_gemm_wmma_lds_tiled (prefill mHC pre-mix GEMM, M=24, K=20480)`
- idea: Re-tile the WMMA GEMM for narrow M: 256-thread WGs, one 16-column n-tile per WG (grid (1, ceil(B/16)) = 32 WGs at B=512 instead of 8), all 8 waves stream the X/W K-chunk (BK=128) with b128 loads, PF=2 chunks in flight, double-buffered LDS, 2 waves own the wmma chains. Same 1280-long wmma_f32_16x16x16 chain per output in the same K order with the same f16 fragments => bit-identical. Symbol f16_gemm_narrow_n16_bk128_pf2 in cand_gemm.hip (5 other NT/BK/PF variants measured: bk64 20-40% slower, n32 slower). Leverage: 2 calls x 20 layers x 2 lanes = 80 calls per 1024-row chunk x (478 - 80) us = 31.8 ms/chunk; 100K prefill = 98 chunks = 3.1 s of 160 s = ~1.9% (an upper bound: the dGPU is not the prefill pole; the mhc_pre_attn stage was 6.9 s). Also 7x at the replay-sized B=65..128 (393-421 -> 58-63 us). ATT of the candidate: 93% of its stall is s_wait_loadcnt, i.e. now memory-bound at 84-90% of the 67 us roof.
- engineer: 475.60 -> 80.20 us, x5.930, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; kb::compare_f32 vs the production kernel on identical inputs: bit_diff=0 at B = 65, 100, 128, 512, 1024 (all 6 variants), every run (r1-r3). Requires out_dim <= 32 and K % 128 == 0 (mHC: 24, 20480); the kernel returns early otherwise, so the Rust dispatch must route only narrow-M calls to it (the M=384 router GEMM keeps the old kernel).
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.00, est_prefill_pct=1.90; integration_cost=small

In F16Matvec::gemm_batched_wmma (crates/v4flash-kernels/src/f16.rs:79) dispatch to it ONLY when n_rows <= 32 && k % 128 == 0 with grid (1, ceil(batch/16)) x 256; the kernel's early return on other shapes must not be relied on: gate in Rust (an unguarded misroute silently writes nothing). Assert or document 16-byte alignment of x and w (b128 loads); flat is 256-B arena-carved and weights are hipMalloc, so it holds today. Expected effect: ~400 us saved per call, 160 calls per 1024-row chunk (40 layers x 2 lanes x pre-attn+pre-ffn), ~64 ms/chunk, up to ~3.9% of a 100K prefill (upper bound; dGPU is not the prefill pole); verify with the mhc_pre_attn / mhc_pre_ffn stage timers after deploy. Leave rms_norm_weighted_quantize_q8 (single-row forward_layer.rs path) alone. #### `F_mhc_glue/router_mv_h20` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `f16_matvec_batched (decode router logits, W 384x5120 f16, grid (48,1,b)x256)`
- idea: Keep one wave per row and the exact per-lane element sequence (i = lane + 32 j, one f32 accumulator, the same h2f(w)*x FMA expression) but hoist the loads: k % (32*CH) == 0 (5120: yes) => CH=20 elements per lane per chunk loaded into register arrays (u16 + f32) with the next chunk's loads issued before the current chunk's FMAs (double buffer); else the production loop. Bit-exact because only the schedule changes. Measured COLD W (production regime, 32 rotating W copies > MALL): b=1 21.7 -> 10.4 us, b=4 21.9 -> 13.4 us; warm b=1 20.3 -> 5.5, b=4 20.8 -> 8.7. CH=40 equal within noise; CH=80 spills (332 B scratch) and loses. Leverage: (21.8 - 10.4..13.4) = 8.4-11.4 us x 80 lane-layers = 0.7-0.9 ms per decode step. Cold it is at 60% of the 640 GB/s roof (DRAM latency x 384 waves on 64 CUs); a non-bit-exact b128-load version could reach ~7 us (not built).
- engineer: 21.70 -> 10.40 us, x2.090, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; kb::compare_f32 bit_diff=0 vs production for k = 5120 (hoisted path) and k = 4992 (generic path), b = 1 and 4, 3 seeds each, all 3 CH variants, in each of 3 runs (150/150 CMPs incl. topk). Note the same symbol serves other f16 matvec_batched callers (compressor / indexer f16 paths) — bit-exact there too by construction, but re-measure before switching them.

#### `F_mhc_glue/topk_wfred` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `router_topk_par (decode, production config: cache-prior, n_protect 2, orig_sel, range_out)`
- idea: Keep the 512-thread layout (thread tid owns expert tid) but make each argmax pass one hardware wave reduction per wave (__ockl_wfred_max_u64 = DPP/permlane, no LDS tree) on a 64-bit sort key (monotonic(score) << 32 | ~tid, so max key == production's predicate: strictly greater wins, ties -> lower index, invalid last), then lane 0 parks the wave max in a pass-parity LDS slot, ONE __syncthreads, every thread merges the 16 slots itself (uniform winner, no second barrier), the owner masks its score. One barrier per pass instead of ten. Same signature and grid. Production config: b=1 11.15 -> 9.20 us, b=4 11.15 -> 9.05 (-18%); plain (prefill) config neutral (7.3 -> 7.3) and b=512 plain a 10% loss (33.0 -> 36.5) => dispatch on b (decode only). Leverage: -2.0 us x 80 lane-layers = 0.16 ms per decode step. The remaining ~9 us is ~3 us launch floor + 12 barriers + the sqrt(softplus) chain.
- engineer: 11.15 -> 9.20 us, x1.210, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; selected / orig_sel / weights / range_out identical to production on 4 seeds (3 random, 1 tie-heavy quantized-logit set with zero bias) at b = 1, 4 and 512, plain and prior forms; 3 runs. Caveat stated in the file: the sort key orders -0.0 below +0.0 where production's == treats them equal; a -0.0 score cannot occur (sqrt(softplus) is +0 or positive; x + y == -0 needs both -0). NaN logits are undefined in both.
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.16, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=drop-in

Harness launches the same symbol router_topk_par, grid (b)x512 (ROUTER_MAX_EXPERTS=512), and the production argument set read from crates/v4flash-kernels/src/het/forward_prefill.rs:6539 (n_expert 384, n_used 6, bias, n_alt 0, prior, n_protect 2, prior_dry 0, orig_sel, range_out). One regime nit, not flattering: production launches this top-k DIRECTLY on de.compute after cap.end() (FP:6466) + an async prior H2D copy, not inside the captured graph, while the engineer timed graph mode; my direct-launch numbers show the same ~18% gain (b=1 11.30->9.34, b=4 11.06->9.13, b=8 10.87->9.02 us). Warm inputs are the production regime for logits (just written by the matvec) and bias; the freshly-copied 1.5 KB prior could miss L2 in production, but both kernels read it at the same point in phase 1, so the delta is unaffected. Re-run: 3 scheduler jobs (review/F_mhc_glue) running the engineer's exact binary+args (./harness_gfx1201 topk 1 prior / topk 4 prior / topk 1). b=1 prior graph base 11.15/10.68/10.70 -> cand 9.25/8.90/8.88 (ratio 0.830/0.833/0.829, median speedup 1.205); b=4 prior graph 11.13/10.81/10.85 -> 9.03/8.81/8.88 (1.226). Plain b=1 neutral (0.98-1.02), plain b=512 direct 31.8 -> 35.0 us = 10% loss, exactly as the engineer reported. p10s consistent across runs. Claimed 1.21 reproduced within 1%. Correctness: engineer's harness 60/60 CMPs per run bit-exact. My independent harness (review/topk_wfred/rev_topk.cpp) 243/243 config x input-class combinations identical on ALL outputs (selected, weights, orig_sel, range, alts, alt_w; outputs poisoned before each launch): b = 1/3/4/5/8/512/1024, n_expert 512 (no padded lanes)/256/100/33/7, n_used 1 and 8, n_alt 1..4 with and without alt_w, prior_dry=1, n_protect 0/2/6/9(>n_used), plain form with orig_sel/range; input classes: random, 7-level ties with zero bias, ALL-EQUAL scores (pure index tie-break -> 0,1,2,3..), all -INF logits (p=0, bias-only ranking), extreme logits -1000/-100/-25/-10/0/10/25/100 (every softplus branch, exact p=0), injected -0.0 in logits/bias/prior (the engineer's stated caveat), large bias and prior, 1-ulp near-ties. Bit-exact everywhere; the -0.0 caveat is unreachable in practice as the engineer argued and did not bite even when forced. Traps checked: same signature and grid (drop-in, no extra kernel); all outputs written; no bounds check dropped (valid = tid < n_expert, padded lanes key 0 = production's -1 sentinel; n_used>8 additionally clamped, Rust already rejects it); wave32 confirmed for both (RT_WAVES=16 layout; it would also stay correct under wave64 since every (tid&31)==0 lane writes the same full-wave max); 37 VGPRs, no scratch; pass-parity LDS double-buffering is race-free (each slot parity is reused only after an intervening barrier). Arithmetic caveat: -2.0 us x 80 lane-layers = 0.16 ms/step is an UPPER bound: V4.1 has 40 routed layers (N_HASH_LAYERS=0) x 2 lanes, but the prior form only runs on layers where box 2 owns experts (plain form gains ~0), and the two lanes overlap in multistream so their savings do not simply add. Real step-level effect is far below the ~8% E2E noise floor; this is a small, real, bit-exact kernel win, not a step-time lever.
  - merge notes: Integration: add router_topk_wfred (cand_topk2.hip body) to crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/router_topk_par.hip (same hsaco module) and in crates/v4flash-kernels/src/router_topk.rs launch_batched_ex (line 322) pick the symbol by b: wfred for b <= ~64 (decode), keep router_topk_par for larger b (prefill b=512 loses 10% with wfred; the crossover between 8 and 512 was not measured, so either measure b=16/32/64/128 or dispatch at b <= 8). Also decide for the single-row launch() at line 141 (grid 1: wfred applies). Keep the kernel dGPU-only as today (INVENTORY: dG). Tests to run under cargo (not run here per the rules): router_topk_alts, router_learned, readback_pack, the mhc/decode fidelity tests, plus the Rust-level equivalence test that selected/weights/orig_sel/range/alts/alt_w match the par kernel on the tie-heavy and all-equal inputs. Nothing else changes (same signature, same grid, same LDS-free assumptions; LDS drops from 10.4 KB to 2.4 KB). Expected effect: -2 us per router_topk call in the prior form, at most 0.16 ms per decode step; do not expect to see it in E2E tok/s.

#### `F_mhc_glue/topk_wave` — measured_loss
- kernel: `router_topk_par`
- idea: Single-wave register top-k: phase 1 by the whole block (or by the one wave in the block-32 flavour), then wave 0 holds the 384 scores as 12 registers per lane and each argmax pass is a 16-slot per-lane scan + a 5-step __shfl_xor butterfly on (value, index) pairs, winner masked in-register, no barriers. Bit-exact, but SLOWER at decode: b=1 prior 15.9 vs 11.1 us, plain 14.2 vs 7.2; block-32 flavour 23.0 / 19.4. Only at b=512 with block 32 does it win (19.95 vs 32.8 us, 512 tiny blocks overlap). Cause: __shfl_xor is ds_bpermute (LDS round trip ~100+ cycles) and a lone wave's dependent chain (12 passes x (16 compares + 10 bpermutes)) is longer than production's 9-level 512-thread tree. This finding led to topk_wfred.
- engineer: 11.15 -> 16.00 us, x0.700, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; selected / orig_sel / weights / range identical to production on 4 seeds incl. ties, b = 1, 4, 512, both flavours, 3 runs.
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.00, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=drop-in
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/F_mhc_glue/cand_topk.hip (router_topk_wave block 512, router_topk_wave_b2 block 32); results/topk_*_r{1,2,3}.txt
- repro: `same job as topk_wfred: bash jobs/mv_topk.sh r1 (harness mode topk lists both files)`

#### `F_mhc_glue/collapse_prefill` — measured_win — review: REFUTED
- kernel: `prefill mHC chain tail: hc_weighted_sum_batched + carry D2D copy + rms_norm_weighted_batched`
- idea: Wire the EXISTING production kernel mhc_fast_batched in collapse-only mode (n_mix 0, do_collapse 1, grid (1,1,B)) for the prefill chain (b > 8 today runs the three separate steps). Measured at B=512 direct: 37.7 us warm / 71.8 flushed vs 58.7 + 34.6 + copy (~93) warm / 74.3 + 36.0 (~110) flushed, and the harness confirms it is bit-exact vs hcw + rmsw at B=512 (documented for b <= 8 in tests/mhc_arena_bitexact.rs). Leverage: 40-55 us x 80 calls per 1024-row chunk = 3-4.4 ms/chunk = ~0.3-0.4 s per 100K prefill (~0.2%). Rust-only change (call MhcArena::launch_fast collapse-only for the prefill chain; keep the carry := split order, which the kernel already handles via its counter/fence protocol).

- correctness: bit_exact=True; kb::compare_f32 of norm_out vs the hcw + rmsw pair at B=512: bit_diff=0 (results/base_prefill_512_warm_r2.txt CMP line); no new kernel.

- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/F_mhc_glue && bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 400 --label F_mhc_glue/baseline --timeout 300 -- bash jobs/base_all.sh r2`
- REVIEW (REFUTED): reran x1.130 runs=9 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=False
  - issues: REFUTED AS STATED: the mechanism is real and bit-exact, but the claimed 2.47x (93 -> 37.7 us) and the 3-4.4 ms/chunk leverage do not survive an honest measurement; the production-regime speedup is 1.13x (~14 us/call). What holds: (1) Kernel/flags/launch fidelity: my review/build.sh rebuilt mhc_fast, hc_weighted_sum, rms_norm, rms_norm_no_weight, f16_gemm_wmma from the unmodified in-tree sources with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco; all five hsacos are byte-identical to the engineer's. The harness launch of mhc_fast_batched (grid (1,1,B)x256, 23 args, n_mix 0/do_collapse 1/write_carry 0/mode 0) matches MhcArena::launch_fast(mix=None, collapse=Some) exactly; hcw grid (20,1,B)x256 with w_stride=24 and rmsw grid (B)x256 match head.rs launch_batched and rms_norm.rs launch_weighted_batched; the D2D copy is the same hipMemcpyAsync DtoD as copy_from_buffer_async. Premise verified in forward_prefill.rs: at b=512 fast_attn (needs mhc_pre_scaled_for(b), b<=8) and fast_ffn (needs mhc_narrow_fallback_for(b), b<=64) are both off, mhc_split needs b<=16, so production runs rms_nw -> gemm -> sinkhorn -> hcw -> copy -> rmsw. (2) Engineer's exact repro (their binary, prefill 512 warm/flush) re-run 3x via the scheduler (review/results/eng_prefill_512_*_rv{1,2,3}.txt): 58.6-59.0 + 34.4-34.6 vs 37.7-38.4 warm, 74.3-74.8 + 35.7-35.9 vs 71.7-72.1 flushed; CMP bit_diff=0 every run. Their numbers reproduce. (3) Correctness beyond the claim: my harness compares cur, norm AND the carry after the copy (NaN-prefilled outputs), at B = 1, 2, 5, 8, 9, 65, 100, 512, 513, 1023, 1024, two input classes (engineer's ranges; residual +-50 with signed carry weights): 66/66 CMPs bit-exact in each of 3 runs, reference finite and nonzero. The collapse WG owns the same elements (tid + 256 j), same f32 dot order over h, same double partials, same 256-wide tree, same rsqrtf finalize: bit-exact by construction. No bounds checks are dropped (NE % 256 == 0 is a compile-time constant; production n is always N_EMBD). Why the claim is refuted: (a) Warm-cache flattery via kb::ab's fixed cyclic variant order in the engineer's prefill mode. The order is [rms_nw, gemm, sink, hcw, rmsw, CHAIN, collapse]: hcw always runs right after rms_nw -> GEMM, whose 42 MB `flat` write + read evicts most of the 42 MB residual from the 64 MB MALL (hcw = 58.7 us, bimodal p10 41), while the collapse-only variant always runs right after CHAIN, which ends by touching the residual (fresh: 37.7 us). In my symmetric harness (review/harness_review.cpp, order [hcw, rmsw, copy, TRIO, collapse, CAND]) warm hcw is 36.7-37.1 us, i.e. the same as the collapse kernel. (b) The candidate still needs the carry := split D2D copy: with n_mix 0 / write_carry 0 the kernel returns before the counter/tail code and never writes carry (mhc_fast.hip 'if (n_mix == 0u) return; // collapse only: no split, no counter'); the wrapper even rejects write_carry without a mix. The claim's 'the kernel already handles [the carry order] via its counter/fence protocol' is wrong for this mode. The copy costs 2.9 us inside the chain warm and ~10 us in the production regime. (c) Timing excluded the copy on the candidate side but included a '(~93)' estimate for the baseline. Measured honestly (3 runs each, medians; review/results/review_all_rv*.txt and review_prod_rv*.txt): B=512 pure warm TRIO(hcw+copy+rmsw) 71.7 vs CAND(collapse+copy) 41.7 = 1.72x; flush40MB inner=3 (2/3 warm) 103.4 vs 69.5 = 1.49x; fully cold inner=1 167.0 vs 165.5 = 1.01x (the collapse kernel streams a cold residual SLOWER than hcw: 155 vs 137 us, which eats the saved rmsw re-read); PRODUCTION regime (untimed rms_nw + M=24 GEMM prefix, exactly FP:3644/3717 before FP:3749, then one timed call) 124.6 vs 110.6 = 1.13x, 14 us/call. B=1024: warm 236 vs 219, prod 247 vs 216 = 1.14x. (d) Leverage arithmetic: the engineer used 20 layers (80 calls/1024-row chunk); N_LAYER is 40 under v41, so 160 calls. 14 us x 160 = 2.2 ms per 1024-row chunk = ~0.22 s of a ~160 s 100K prefill = ~0.14%, versus the claimed 3-4.4 ms/chunk. Also note the honest per-call gain is smaller than the ~2x call-count undercount, so the net leverage is roughly what was claimed in ms but for the wrong reasons and still ~0.1% of prefill. Rules followed: all GPU work through gpu_submit.sh/gpu_wait.sh (--label review/F_mhc_glue, 9 jobs, --mb 400), no cargo, no in-tree edits, engineer's files untouched; queue empty at the end. Reviewer files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/F_mhc_glue/review/{harness_review.cpp,build.sh,jobs/*.sh,results/*.txt}; verdict line appended to _infra/WINS.log.
  - merge notes: If someone still wants the ~2 ms/chunk (bit-exact, Rust-only, low risk): in forward_prefill.rs, in the non-split, non-fast branches of both chains (pre-attn after hc_sinkhorn at ~FP:3730, pre-ffn after ~FP:6355), replace `de.hc_weighted.launch_batched(... &bd.hc_pre_carry ...)` + `de.rms_w.launch_weighted_batched(...)` with `de.mhc_arena.launch_fast(&de.compute, None, Some(FastCollapse{ x: &bd.residual (or &bd.after_attn_hc), cur_out: &mut sd.attn_cur (or sd.ffn_cur), norm_out: &mut sd.attn_input_norm (or bd.ffn_input_norm), norm_w: &dlw.attn_norm (or ffn_norm) }), &mut bd.hc_pre_carry, false, HC_DIM, RMS_EPS, SINKHORN_ITERS, SINKHORN_EPS, b)`, and KEEP the existing `hc_pre_carry.copy_from_buffer_async(&split)` after it (the collapse-only launch never writes the carry; write_carry must stay false since there is no mix). Keep the CED KvSourceOnly guard around the copy and the subtensor dump of attn_cur. Wrapper preconditions already hold at b=512/1024 (carry [B,24], residual [B,HC_DIM], cur/norm [B,N_EMBD], norm_w len N_EMBD; no counters/inv_rows needed since mix=None). Gate it behind a knob (e.g. V41_MHC_FAST_PREFILL, default on) for rollback. Tests: extend tests/mhc_arena_bitexact.rs beyond b<=8 to b=512/513/1024 comparing cur, norm and carry (the reviewer harness shows bit-exact there, but the in-tree test only covers 1..=8), plus the CED replay path (seed_carry) at b>8. Measure the real gain on a 100K prefill via the evtrace k.mhc_pre_attn.hc_weighted/rms_w stage times rather than the harness number: expect ~14 us x 160 calls per 1024-row chunk (~0.1% of prefill), not 3-4 ms/chunk. **Dead ends (F_mhc_glue):** (1) topk_wave (cand_topk.hip): a single wave doing the K argmax passes in registers with __shfl_xor butterflies is bit-exact but 1.4-2x SLOWER than the 512-thread LDS tree at decode (b=1 prior 15.9 vs 11.1 us; plain 14.2 vs 7.2; block-32 flavour 23 / 19 us) because __shfl_xor compiles to ds_bpermute (an LDS round trip per step) and the lone wave's dependent chain (12 passes x (16-slot scan + 10 bpermutes)) is longer than nine parallel tree levels; it only wins at b=512 with 32-thread blocks (20 vs 33 us) where 512 tiny blocks overlap. Replaced by topk_wfred (hardware wave reduction + one barrier per pass), which wins 18% in the production config but is neutral plain and a 10% loss at b=512. (2) router matvec CH=80 (all 160 elements per lane in two chunks): 256 VGPRs, 332 B scratch, 87 spills -> equal to baseline at b=1 and 2.4x slower at b=4; CH=40 no better than CH=20 cold. (3) GEMM variants with BK=64 (20-40% slower: more LDS stores per byte) and 32-column n-tiles (16 WGs instead of 32, 8-20% slower). (4) mv + topk fusion: not adjacent in one graph (stage boundary + host prior copy between them), so a kernel fusion cannot remove a graph node without Rust restructuring. (5) Not targets (measured at or above 90% of roof / at the ~3 us launch floor): rms_norm_no_weight_batched B=512, hc_weighted_sum_batched B=512, rms_norm_weighted_batched B=512, hc_post_from_split, vec_add_inplace, head hc_weighted_sum, mhc_fast pre-ffn collapse.

**Notes (F_mhc_glue):** Baseline = the unmodified in-tree .hip compiled with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco for gfx1201 and launched with the wrappers' exact grid/args (NOTES.md section 0 lists every geometry, read from mhc_arena.rs / f16.rs / router_topk.rs / head.rs / rms_norm.rs / forward_prefill.rs). All numbers are from the resumed run with the production hub DOWN (idle dGPU); the first attempt's graph-mode decode numbers (results/*_r1.txt from 03:5x) were polluted by the live hub and are superseded by *_r2.txt. Decode kernels timed as captured graphs of 20 calls (production replays stage graphs), direct launch as the secondary number; prefill kernels direct-launched (production launches them directly), warm and with a 40 MB flush. The router W cold regime was produced by rotating over 32 W copies (125 MB > 64 MB MALL + 8 MB L2) inside the captured graph; the baseline matvec is identical warm and cold (latency chain), the candidate is 2x slower cold than warm, so the cold numbers are the production-relevant ones. Two departures from production to note: (a) the decode harness times rms_norm_weighted_batched at n=5120, which decode fuses into mhc_fast; the production decode shapes (n=1280 q_a, n=512 kv/comp) are measured in the rms mode and used for the leverage arithmetic; (b) synthetic uniform inputs (logits in [-3,3], W in +-0.05 / +-0.02) — bit-exactness is structural (same fp expressions / same reduction order / total-order argmax), not input-dependent, and the tie-heavy top-k seed exercises the tie rules. est_ms_per_token figures are per 8-row decode step (2 lanes x 40 layers = 80 lane-layers; head prep x 8 rows), stated as such in each idea; est_prefill_pct is of the 160 s 100K-prefill wall and for the GEMM is an upper bound because the dGPU is not the prefill pole. ATT profiles of both baselines and winners are under results/att_*/ (gemm, gemmc, mv, mvc; the topk traces did not parse). No cargo, no in-tree edits, no GPU work outside the scheduler; nothing left running (queue empty at return). Files: NOTES.md (running notes + summary table), build.sh, harness.cpp, cand_gemm.hip, cand_rms.hip, cand_router_mv.hip, cand_topk.hip, cand_topk2.hip, jobs/{base_all,cands,winners,mv_topk,att_cands}.sh, repro.sh, results/. Integration hint: the GEMM candidate must be dispatched only for out_dim <= 32 && K % 128 == 0 (mHC pre-mix); the router M=384 GEMM at prefill keeps f16_gemm_wmma_lds_tiled. topk_wfred should be dispatched for b <= 64 only (it loses at b=512).

#### `C1_dense_decode/tB_compile_time_batch` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `q8_0_gemv_bpack_warp8 + q8_0_grouped_gemv_bpack`

#### `C1_dense_decode/quantize_grid_pad` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `q8_0_quantize_f32 (S/q8_0.rs:110 quantize_input_batched)`
- idea: Launch grid = blocks + 1 (one idle WG; the kernel's `if (b >= blocks) return` guard makes it a no-op). q8_0_quantize_f32 takes 16.7-18.8 us instead of 4-8 us whenever the block count is exactly 2046-2048 or 4096 WGs: K=32768 (the wo_a input, FP:6126) at b=2 and b=4 per lane, K=8192 (wo_b input) at b=8, 16384x4, 65536x1. Ruled out: address aliasing (x/xq/xscale offsets of 256 B-128 KB: no change), graph capture (plain launches identical), element count (32767x2 slow, 32800x2 fast). With +1 WG: K=32768 b=2 16.8 -> 5.85 us, b=4 18.6 -> 8.2 us (4.5 with the wave kernel); GEMV grids that are powers of two (qb 4096 WGs, wo_a 1024, kv 64) are not affected. Arithmetic: one launch per lane-layer at b=2/4 is ~10-11 us too slow -> 0.41-0.44 ms/step per lane, 0.82 ms dGPU at 2 x 4 rows.

(1) Baseline fidelity: rebuilt base_q8_gfx1201.hsaco from the unmodified in-tree crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/q8_0_matvec.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco --offload-arch=gfx1201 is byte-identical to the engineer's; harness launches the same symbol q8_0_quantize_f32 with grid (blocks,1,1) x block 32 and args (xq, xscale, x, blocks), which is exactly q8_0.rs quantize_input_batched -> hipModuleLaunchKernel (v4flash-hip/src/module.rs:114); production site FP:6126 quantizes Q_FLAT=32768 x b rows on the dp4a arm (b<=64), so grids are 1024*b: b=2 -> 2048, b=4 -> 4096 as claimed. Regime (warm activations, graph mode) matches production. (2) Reproduction: engineer's exact repro (submit_quant_grid.sh shapes, their binary) x3 separate runs: K=32768 b=4 gpad0 medians 19.25/18.49/18.46 vs gpad1 8.20/8.12/8.14 -> 18.49/8.14 = 2.27x (claim 2.27); b=2 16.82 -> 5.82 (2.89x). Rebuilt-harness run identical (16.78/18.50 -> 5.79/8.13). My own same-process interleaved harness (review/gpad_check, grid vs +1/+7/+64) x3 + no-graph: same numbers. rocprofv3 kernel-trace hardware timestamps: the dispatch itself is 14.24 us at grid 2048 vs 3.40 us at 2049, and 17.08 vs 5.68 at 4096/4097 - so it is not a graph, launch-overlap or event artefact. With a producer kernel (fill of x) between consecutive quantizes (production-like, no back-to-back identical dispatches) the penalty persists: b=2 20.0 -> 8.8, b=4 20.4 -> 11.1 us. (3) Correctness: padded vs unpadded outputs byte-identical (xq) and bit-identical (xscale) at 22 shapes incl. blocks 1, 32, 2047, 2049, 4095, 4097, 8192, b=1..8 at K=32768 and K=8192, K=5120/2304, inputs with an all-zero block (d=0 path), a 1e30 spike (clamp) and 1e-30 values; outputs pre-filled 0xAA show every element/scale written; base matches a CPU reference of the kernel math bit-exactly. Note the engineer's harness never compared padded vs unpadded base directly (both were compared to the wave kernel); my direct comparison closes that. (4) Engineer's model of the pathology is incomplete: it is NOT 'exactly 2046-2048 or 4096'. grid 2047 and 4095 are equally slow (16.8/18.4 us), 2047+1 = 2048 stays slow, and 3072 (b=3) is bimodal (median 10.4-11.5 vs 6.95 padded; trace p90 16.4 us). So 'blocks+1' is not a general rule; it happens to work for every production grid 1024*b (b=1..8 all measured: +1 fixes b=2,3,4 and is neutral at 1,5,6,7,8) and 256*b (only b=8 affected). Mechanism remains unexplained. (5) est_ms_per_token 0.42 is arithmetically right (10.4-11 us x 40 layers) but applies only to a lane with exactly 2 or 4 rows; b=3 gives ~0.15-0.19 ms; b=1 and b=5 gain nothing. It is dGPU device time; the 1:1 step-time conversion assumes the dense chain is on the critical path. Other quantize sites (5120, 2304, 1280, 6144, 8192 at b<=5) never land in the window at decode.
  - merge notes: One-line change in crates/v4flash-kernels/src/q8_0.rs quantize_input_batched (and quantize_input for symmetry): grid (total_blocks + 1, 1, 1); the kernel's `if (b >= blocks) return` guard makes the extra WG a no-op and the `blocks` argument must stay total_blocks. Bit-exact, so no KLD gate needed. Because the mechanism is unknown and the slow window is wider than the engineer thinks (2047/2048, ~3072, 4095/4096), add a regression microbench asserting q8_0_quantize_f32 at grid 1024*b+1 for b=1..8 and 256*b+1 stays <= ~1.2x of the neighbouring grid; prefer a pad that lands well outside the window (e.g. +7 or +64, both measured neutral) over +1 if the wrapper ever pads grids other than 1024*b. Alternatively adopt the engineer's q8_0_quantize_f32_wave kernel, which is faster still (4.5 us at b=4) but is a new kernel and was slow at 256/512 WGs without the pad too, so it also needs the pad. Verify in the live server via the k.output_proj.grouped_matvec stage event at 2/4 rows vs 1/5 rows per lane before and after. Prefill (512/1024-row chunks -> 524288+ WGs) was not tested; do not assume other windows do not exist there.

  - issues: Baseline fidelity: my base_q8_gfx1201.hsaco (unmodified crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/q8_0_matvec.hip, $KFLAGS_V41 --genco --offload-arch=gfx1201) is byte-identical to the engineer's; the harness launches the production symbol q8_0_quantize_f32 with grid=(blocks,1,1) x block=32 and args (xq, xscale, x, blocks), exactly what q8_0.rs quantize_input / quantize_input_batched do. Warm activations + graph mode is the right regime (the input was just written by the preceding node; decode stages are graph-captured); both variants are timed the same way (10-node graph / 10, interleaved, 60 rounds). Repro: engineer's exact 3rd submit_r3.sh ticket, 3 separate scheduler jobs (review/C1_dense_decode): K=5120 b=4 base 4.52/4.46/4.46 us -> cand 3.60/3.55/3.56 (median 4.46 -> 3.56 = 1.253x vs claimed 1.26x); b=8 5.08/5.00/5.01 -> 3.70/3.66/3.64; K=8192 b=4 4.74/4.70/4.71 -> 3.61/3.59/3.58; K=1280 b=4 3.90/3.86/3.85 -> 3.50/3.46/3.48; K=2304 b=4 3.96/3.95/3.96 -> 3.47/3.48/3.48 -- all within 1-2% of the engineer's r3-r5. Shapes the engineer did not time: b=1 at K=1280/2304/5120/8192/32768 = 1.08/1.09/1.10/1.12/1.33x; b=5 (max production b per lane) = 1.11/1.16/1.26/1.39/1.95x (K=32768 b=5 9.28 -> 4.76 us); K=5120 b=3 1.20x; tails K=224 and K=1312 (7 and 41 blocks) 1.08x. Correctness: xq int8-exact and xscale bit-exact vs production on all 7 repro shapes x 3 runs, and in my odd.cpp on 18 cases: 1/7/9/41/6149 blocks (WG tails), b=1/2/3/5/8 at K=5120/8192/32768, and adversarial blocks (all-zero, all-equal +/-, one 1e30 value, denormals only, exact .5 ties with d=1, negative amax, 3.4e38, signed zero, +inf, -inf, a single NaN, +/-127 exactly, 126.5 rounding boundary, clamp boundary); a 4 KB sentinel after xq and xscale is untouched (no out-of-range writes); base also matches a CPU model of its own arithmetic. Only deviation found: an ALL-NaN block yields xscale=NaN in the candidate vs 0 in production (production's serial fmaxf(0, NaN) drops NaNs; the shfl_xor tree keeps NaN only when every lane is NaN); xq is identical even then; irrelevant unless activations are already NaN. Candidate does the full work (every element and scale written, b>=blocks guard kept, no alignment assumption beyond 4-byte floats, no extra kernel). ISA: 12 VGPR / 7 SGPR vs 21/54, wave32. Estimate caveats: (1) the wave kernel does NOT fix the 2048/4096-block pathology on its own (K=32768 b=2 16.74 -> 16.50, b=4 18.5 -> 17.6 us in my runs); the claimed 8.2 -> 4.5 us at K=32768 appears only with the grid+1 pad (I reproduced 8.17 -> 4.49 with C1_GPAD=1), so the 0.26 ms/step figure is joint with the separately-reviewed pad claim and with dropping the duplicate FP:3979 quantize; standalone the win is ~4-5 us per lane-layer at b=4 (6-7 launches) -> ~0.17-0.2 ms/step for one lane, i.e. arithmetically right but small in absolute terms. (2) 'drop-in' is slightly overstated: the two wrapper LaunchConfigs must change (grid ceil(blocks/8) x 256).
  - merge notes: Integration: add q8_0_quantize_f32_wave (from scratch/kernel_ideas/C1_dense_decode/cand_bpack.hip lines 236-251) to crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/q8_0_matvec.hip and switch both q8_0.rs wrappers (quantize_input, quantize_input_batched) to get_function("q8_0_quantize_f32_wave") with LaunchConfig grid=((blocks+7)/8, 1, 1), block=(256,1,1), same args (xq, xscale, x, blocks); every call site (forward_prefill.rs 1828/1882/3811/3882/3979/6126/6161, forward_layer.rs, forward_head.rs, mtp.rs, Engram 3507) goes through those two wrappers, so no call-site edits. Combine with the grid-pad fix for the K=32768 b=2/4 case (e.g. grid = (blocks+7)/8 + 1, which my C1_GPAD=1 run shows is fast for the wave kernel too: 4.49 us at b=4, 3.84 at b=2) -- the wave kernel alone still hits the 16-18 us pathology at those grids. Kernel assumes wave32 (threadIdx.x >> 5, 8 waves per 256-thread WG): true for gfx1201 and gfx1151 under build.rs flags; add a static_assert/warpSize check if the kernel is ever compiled for a wave64 target. Tests: run crates/v4flash-kernels/tests/q8_0_matvec.rs (quantize oracle) and a bit-exact xq/xscale diff old-vs-new at production shapes including a tail (blocks % 8 != 0) -- no KLD fidelity gate needed since outputs are bit-identical; document the all-NaN-block xscale difference (NaN vs 0). Keep the old kernel symbol in the .hip until the roofline bench (tests/bench_v41_kernel_roofline.rs) is updated. Expected step-time effect is small (~0.2 ms/step at one lane b=4 standalone, ~0.26-0.28 with the pad and the FP:3979 duplicate removal); do not double-count with the grid-pad claim in the ledger. Reviewer artifacts: scratch/kernel_ideas/C1_dense_decode/review/quantize_wave/ (build.sh, submit.sh, wait_all.sh, odd.cpp, results/repro_r{1,2,3}.txt, extra.txt, extra_gpad1.txt, odd.txt).

#### `C1_dense_decode/two_lane_merged_dense` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `q8_0_gemv_bpack_warp8 (all dense sites) at >= 6 rows`
- idea: One tB(b0+b1) launch over both lanes' rows (xq/out contiguous) reads each dense weight once instead of once per lane (FP:2753-2762). Kernel-level price, cold, inner=1, 3 runs: at 4+4 rows the merged launch costs 0.45-0.52 of the two production launches on the >= 7 MB shapes (q_a 27.8 vs 61.3 us; wo_b 90.7 vs 211; qb 91.9 vs 182; down 38.5 vs 79.9; gate 3+3: 35.2 vs 79) and 0.81-0.85 of two tB4 launches on the small ones (launch floor ~13 us each). Summed over the 8 sites vs 2 x tB4: ~315 us/layer -> ~12.6 ms of dGPU time per step at 8 rows (+1.8 ms Engram/head). NOT a step-time estimate: the lanes are separate graphs on separate streams offset in time to overlap box-2; each merged site needs a cross-lane join (event pair 5-10 us x ~10 sites/layer = up to 4 ms/step back) and it removes the pipelining the lanes exist for. Must be priced in the pipeline.
- engineer: 211.30 -> 90.70 us, x2.330, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; merged output bitexact=YES vs the two per-lane production outputs (b0+b1 in {4+4, 5+5, 3+3}) on q_a, qb, wo_b, gate, down.
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=12.60, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=large
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/C1_dense_decode/cand_bpack.hip (tB6/tB8/tB10); harness.cpp (mode lanes); results/small_r3.txt, small_r4.txt, small_r5.txt (lanes sections)
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/C1_dense_decode && bash submit_r3.sh r6 (3rd ticket; 'lanes' sections) ; bash wait_to.sh <ticket> small_r6.txt`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x2.330 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Kernel-level claim reproduces and the baseline is faithful, but the per-step arithmetic and the framing need corrections. (1) Baseline fidelity: the engineer's base_q8_gfx1201.hsaco is byte-identical (sha256 092c6ad4...) to my rebuild of the UNMODIFIED in-tree q8_0_matvec.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 (= build.rs flags exactly); same symbol q8_0_gemv_bpack_warp8, grid (M/8)x256 = wrapper div_ceil(n_rows,8) x 256, identical argument order/semantics; cold weights + graph mode matches production (StageCap replays on de.compute). (2) Reproduction: engineer's exact 'lanes' commands with their binary, 3 separate scheduler runs: wo_b 4+4 two cold production launches 212.4/211.5/211.6 us vs merged tB8 91.0/90.8/91.1 -> 2.325x (claim 211.3->90.7, 2.33). All other shapes within 1-2% of the engineer's r3-r5 (q_a 0.45, qb 0.50, gate 0.44, down 0.48; kv 4+4 = 0.45, not measured by the engineer). (3) Correctness: bit-exact vs the two per-lane production outputs at 4+4, 5+5, 3+3, and the UNTESTED 4+3 (tB7), 5+4 (tB9), 1+7, 2+6, 5+3, 1+1, and row tails M=1004/1000/520/2312/8 (row guard intact; all b0+b1 rows written); both agree with the CPU reference to rel_rmse 1e-7 with identical bit patterns. NOTE tB7 is MISSING from cand_bpack.hip (only tB1-6,8,10): a 7-row step splits 4+3, so the merged path has no kernel for a production case; I added tB7/tB9 in review/lanes/cand_bpack_rev.hip and they are bit-exact. (4) Traps: (a) MALL flattery checked both ways: with the two lanes' launches back-to-back in ONE graph the second read of a 45 MB weight hits the MALL (tB4+tB4 = 115 us, not 180) and the merged ratio would be only 0.60 vs production / 0.80 vs 2xtB4; but the hub's per-layer order (combine A, chain A, combine B, chain B, FP:2790-2830) puts >100 MB of other weight streams between the two lanes' reads of any site, and a chain-shaped test (q_a,qb,kv,wo_b per lane, 100 MB/lane, one graph, 2 runs) gives merged = 0.474 of the production 2-lane chain (423->200 us) and 0.554 of 2x tB chains (362->200): the engineer's cold+cold regime is the right one. (b) The engineer's '~315 us/layer -> 12.6 ms/step' sums per-site deltas taken as separately-timed one-node graphs, which double-counts a ~13 us graph-launch floor per site against the merged one-node graph; production nodes cost ~2.5 us. Equal-graph accounting gives ~265 us/layer, ~10.6 ms of dGPU time per step at 8 rows (4+4), ~16% less than claimed; still large. (c) est_ms_per_token=12.6 is dGPU device time at the MAX row count only: merged lanes exist only at >=6 rows (typical decode is 1-4 rows -> 0 gain), and it is NOT step time: both lanes' dGPU work already shares de.compute (so no event-pair join cost, contrary to the notes), but today chain A runs on the dGPU while the host waits for lane B's box-2 reply; a merged chain must wait for BOTH replies before starting, so the step-time effect is unmeasured and may be far smaller (cannot be priced with the hub down, no cargo). (d) The candidate needs the tB instantiations (also the engineer's idea 1): the production runtime-loop kernel at b=8 is only 0.61 of two b=4 launches; the 0.43 comes from tB8.
  - merge notes: Integration is LARGE and mostly host-side, and should be staged. (1) Do the tB compile-time-batch kernels first (separate reviewed claim) and add tB7 and tB9 (7-row steps split 4+3; 9 would be 5+4 if lanes ever exceed 8) plus a runtime fallback for any b0+b1 without an instantiation; VGPRs 92-98 at tB7-10. (2) Cheapest, pipeline-free first slice: the head. multistream.rs:941-943 calls head_rows(bd_a) then head_rows(bd_b) sequentially after both forwards; forward_head_batch already stages per-row prep into head_xq_b/head_xscale_b (HEAD_BATCH_MAX=16) -> let it take two (bd, b) sources and run ONE matvec_bpack over b_a+b_b rows: ~1.25 ms of dGPU time per step at 8 rows, bit-exact, no cross-lane dependency. Same for Engram wkv at L1/L14 if its staging allows. (3) For the 8 per-layer sites: production keeps quantized activations in the SHARED scratch sd (sd.xq_n_embd, sd.qr, ...) which lane B's chain overwrites, and stages are graph-captured per lane (stage_cap keyed by lane_ptr); a merged launch needs [b_a+b_b]-row contiguous xq/xscale/out (or a two-base-pointer kernel variant, trivially the same cost) and a joint two-lane pre_moe_chain stage whose graph interleaves merged dense sites with per-lane attention/mHC/router nodes. No events are needed on the dGPU (both lanes are on de.compute), but the merged chain can only start after both lanes' combines, i.e. after both box-2 replies, removing the current overlap of chain A with lane B's box-2 wait; it is also incompatible with V41_MS_STAGGER=1. Gate the merge by rows (>=6) and keep the per-lane path as rollback. (4) Test plan: multistream_step G5-style bit-exact check that merged-lane logits equal the two-lane logits for b=6,7,8 (splits 3+3, 4+3, 4+4), plus a live step-time A/B on real traffic at >=6 rows before believing any ms/step number; expected dGPU-time saving ~265 us/layer at 4+4 (~10.6 ms/step) + head/Engram ~1.8 ms, zero below 6 rows.

#### `C1_dense_decode/lds_staged_activations_b8` — untested
- kernel: `q8_0_gemv_bpack_warp8 (tB8/tB10, merged-lane regime)`
- idea: Stage the B activation rows for a 32-block K-chunk in LDS once per WG (8 waves share it) so each weight block needs 2 DRAM loads + LDS reads instead of 2 + 3B global loads; targets the b=8 falloff (tB8 502-572 GB/s net vs 600-628 at b=1; ATT of tB8: 87% stall, all s_wait_loadcnt on the 26 loads per block). Only relevant if the merged-lane pass (b=8-10) is adopted; b per lane is <=5 in production where tB is already at 560-620 GB/s. Ceiling ~+10% on the merged launches. Not built.
- correctness: bit_exact=True; same math order possible (LDS only changes where the activation bytes come from).
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.00, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=medium
- files: NOTES.md idea 9; results/att_tB8_qa_b8/
- repro: `n/a`

**Dead ends (C1_dense_decode):** (1) K-unroll (ku2/ku4) on top of the compile-time-B kernel: 5-25% slower at b>=4, VGPRs 95-144 cost occupancy and the hardware already pipelines the per-lane blocks; also no gain at b=1 on the latency-bound kv shape. (2) WG geometry 4/16 waves per WG: identical to 8 waves within +-1% everywhere (wave count is fixed by M). (3) The fused shared-expert quantize with 256- or 512-thread WGs (r4/r2, 2-4 rows per wave): 1-4 us slower than the 1024-thread WG at b<=5 (VGPRs 97-166); and the 1024-thread WG loses at b=8 (100 VGPRs -> one 32-wave WG per WGP, 72 WGs on 32 WGPs = tail) where A2 (fused g+u+swiglu + quant_wave) wins instead. (4) The quantize pathology is NOT address aliasing (x/xq/xscale offsets change nothing) and NOT graph capture (plain launches identical); it is the exact WG count (2046-2048, 4096), fixed by grid+1. (5) memset-based cache flush (first attempt) charged 80 MB of dirty-line write-back to the kernel under test (q_a b=1 read 37 us instead of 16); use a clean READ flush. (6) The r2 numbers from the first attempt were taken under a 56-100% busy hub and are superseded by r3-r5 (hub down); they agreed in direction on every point.

**Notes (C1_dense_decode):** Baseline = production 361d4f9 code objects built from the unmodified in-tree q8_0_matvec.hip / q8_0_grouped_matvec.hip / swiglu.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco --offload-arch=gfx1201; launch geometry, args and the M18 split Q8_0 layout copied from S/q8_0.rs / FP call sites. Regime: cold weights via a 72 MB clean read flush before every timed block (rotation over N copies for weights <= 36 MB, inner=1 for the 35-703 MB ones), warm activations, graph mode, 60 interleaved rounds, three separate process runs (r3/r4/r5) that agree within 1-2% on every row; every med printed is GROSS (it includes the per-graph-launch null floor measured in the same run: 3.7-7 us with rotation, 13-15 us at inner=1) and net GB/s in NOTES.md = bytes/(med - null). pct_of_roof above = (bytes/640 GB/s)/(med - null). The hub was DOWN for all r3+ runs (device idle), so head (703 MB) and Engram (167 MB) were measured at full size, not as proxies. Differences from production to keep in mind: synthetic random weights/activations (the GEMVs are data-independent; the fused chain was checked with the clamp path exercised); the shared chain is timed as a graph of only its own nodes (production interleaves other stages); the quantize nodes are timed warm in a 10-node graph (production: one launch each, warm input). The tB VGPR counts are 30/41/65/73/95 at B=1/2/4/5/8 (occupancy still 16/16) vs 51 for the runtime kernel. Total measured, bit-exact, drop-in/small savings at 4 rows (1 lane): tB 3.03 + shared fusion 0.18 + quantize grid pad 0.41 + quantize_wave 0.26 = ~3.9 ms of dGPU time per step (x2 for 2 lanes at 4+4 rows = ~7.8 ms), against the inventory's ~25.5 ms/step byte floor and 2-3x-of-floor measured stages. The two-lane merge is the only lever that halves bytes (~12.6 ms dGPU/step at 8 rows) but must be priced in the pipeline. No in-tree files were modified; all candidates live in scratch/kernel_ideas/C1_dense_decode/{cand_bpack.hip, cand_shared.hip}. No GPU process left running; scheduler queue empty at return.

#### `A_moe_smallb/wl_c8dn2` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `kwide gate/up + kwide2 down chain (package of wl_cap + kwide_c8 + dn2_member_outer)`
- idea: The recommended package: both kernels loop over work items with grid.y capped at 8 (no 6b-bound empty WGs), kwide instantiated with MXFP4_KW_MAX_CHUNK=8 for b<=8 (chunk_size 8), and the member-outer down twin. Chain 400->377 us (b=2), 480->420 (b=4), 620->488 (b=8); b=1 0.964, b=3 0.901, b=5 0.838, b=6 0.812, b=7 0.792; 6-distinct-expert regime 0.915/0.852/0.811. Also the loop kernel with the 6-bound grid at b=1 is 3% slower than production, so use min(bound, 8), never a cap above the bound. (b) A second symbol compiled with -DMXFP4_KW_MAX_CHUNK=8 (e.g. mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_kwide_c8; add the file to build.rs or a #define-renamed include), selected when b <= 8 AND chunk_size = 8 is passed to moe_work_items_builder and to kwide2/dn2 in the same pass (the wrapper's `chunk_size > MXFP4_KW_MAX_CHUNK` check must use 8 for that symbol). Hard-gate on b <= 8 (a group can have up to b members; a split group re-streams gate/up and loses 14% at b=12) - do not rely on V41_MS_SLOTS=8; the box-2 batched pass (merged lanes) and any V41_MS_SLOTS override must fall back to the 32-chunk symbol above 8 rows. WHY REFUTED AS A PRODUCTION WIN: (1) The gate/up loop kernel is intrinsically 4-5% SLOWER than production at the SAME grid with ZERO empties: parts with exact=1 (loop kernel with nullptr count, n_items = gridDim.y = exact): b=1 207.2 vs 197.5 us, b=2 263.4 vs 251.2, b=4 290.5 vs 278.9, b=8 335.1 vs 321.4 (+9-14 us per call at every b; 123 VGPR/84 SGPR vs 119/70). The engineer attributed the b<=2 loss to 'LUT stage per empty'; it is a body regression. The engineer's own results/parts_wl_run1_b4.txt shows it too: cand wl.wl gate/up 292.7 us vs production BOUND grid 288.7 (exact 284.1), i.e. at b=4 the gate/up half of this change is a loss even with 20 empties/tile; the whole b=4 chain gain comes from kwide2. (2) The selection regime flatters the candidate: E=4 ppr=3 makes every row pick from the same 4 experts, so n_wi=4 at every b and empties = 6b-4 per row tile (44 at b=8) while bytes stay at 75 MB. (3) est_ms_per_token 0.4 (b=4) is an E=4 upper bound: at b=4 my chain delta is 6-7 us x 40 = 0.25 ms/step, and it is ~0 in the many-distinct regime; the 1.1 headline needs 44 empties per tile. No warm-cache, flag, skipped-work or timing-exclusion trap found; the chain includes both builders, q8k, memset and reduce.
  - merge notes: Do not merge the gate/up (kwide) half as written: it regresses the no-empty case (hub b=1 lanes, prefill B=512 if the loop lands in the shared symbol) by 4-5% of gate/up time. The salvageable part is the kwide2 (down) loop + cap: my parts show cand wl.wl down equals the exact-grid kwide2 within 1-2% at every b (b=1 101.1 vs 98.9, b=2 133.0 vs 129.7, b=4 164.1 vs 162.3, b=8 207.8 vs 207.4) and beats the bound grid by 18 us at b=4 and 47 us at b=8 in the E=4 regime (fewer empties in production, so less). If integrated: (a) change only K/mxfp4_matvec.hip kwide2 (loop + `n_items = *n_work_items_dev or gridDim.y`), keep the production kwide early-out unless a rewrite of the loop kernel recovers the exact-grid time (check VGPR 123->119 and SGPR 84->70; put the `blockIdx.y >= n_items` uniform early-out BEFORE the LUT global load + barrier so empties stay as cheap as production's); (b) wrapper S/mxfp4.rs:183 grid.y = min(n_work_items, 8) only on the decode/box-2 batched path (b <= 8); prefill B=512 (n_wi ~300-480) must keep the full bound (the loop is a no-op there but the cap must not apply); (c) the wrapper assertion `n_work_items as usize > work_items.len()` stays; add a test in tests/mxfp4_wi_devcount.rs that n_wi > grid.y (looping WGs) is bit-exact vs the full-bound launch, including a stale-entries case (work_items past the count hold earlier layers' items); (d) re-measure on box 2 with a realistic selection (distinct picks per row, pool of 6,160 slots) rather than E=4 shared experts, and on the hub at b=1 (no empties) to confirm no regression; (e) est gain to quote: ~(bound - n_wi) x 320 x ~3 ns per layer for down only, i.e. ~10-20 us/layer at b=4-8 with production n_wi, well under 1 ms/step.

Three separate repro processes (review/results/chain_c8_run{1,2,3}_b{2,4,8}.txt) give chain ratios kwide_c8.c8 vs production chain of 0.939/0.955/0.948 at b=8 (median 0.948 = 1.055x; baseline med 610-621 us, candidate 581-589 us, matching the claimed 615->587), 0.989/0.993/0.988 at b=4 (1.1%, below the README 5% noise floor but consistent in median AND p10 over my 3 + the engineer's 4 runs), 0.996/1.005/1.001 at b=2 (neutral). base.c8 (chunk 8 on the production build) is 1.000-1.003 everywhere, so the delta is the compile-time array/LDS reduction, as claimed. Per-kernel (parts_c8_parts_b8.txt): kwide 353 -> 308.5 us at b=8, 284 -> 275 at b=4 (engineer: 356->307). Baseline fidelity: build.sh compiles the UNMODIFIED in-tree mxfp4_pair_matvec.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco (git diff vs 361d4f9 empty); my rebuilt base_pair and cand_kwide_c8 code objects are sha256-identical to the engineer's; harness launches the same symbol with grid (288, n_wi_bound) x 256 and the exact 17-argument order of mxfp4_pair.rs:288-298; chunk_size 32 = remote_experts.rs CHUNK_SIZE; n_wi bound = dispatch.rs moe_wi_upper_bound with the devcount pointer (V41_MOE_WI_DEVCOUNT defaults ON, so the bound grid IS production); gbound 384 = box-2 group_bound N_EXPERT; weights cold (16-expert 301 MB rotation > 32 MB MALL), activations warm; graph mode matches the hub lanes (box-2 batched pass launches directly, which affects both variants equally). Only trivial deviations: max_per_expert 64 vs production rows (an index stride) and synthetic data. ISA confirms 119->71 VGPR, LDS 16 KB->4 KB. Correctness: bit-exact (bit_diff=0 on mid, partials and out over 8 selections) at every shape I ran: b=1,2,3,4,5,7,8; E=8 ppr=6 (8 distinct experts, every pick real); E=1 ppr=1 at b=8 (a group of exactly 8 members = MXFP4_KW_MAX_CHUNK edge); exact grid. Inputs are random MXFP4 bytes with E8M0 scales and random Q8_K rows, outputs all nonzero/finite, twin chain agrees to rel_rmse 1e-7, so the comparison is not trivially satisfied. Tails: b=3 0.992, b=5 0.971, b=7 0.962; E=8 ppr=6: b=4 0.974, b=8 0.944 (holds with more real work items). CAVEAT on the claimed mechanism: with an EXACT grid.y (no 6b-n_wi empty WGs) the gain at b=8 collapses from 5-6% to 1.5% (556 -> 548 us, chain_c8_exact_b8.txt) and stays 1.2% at b=4. So most of the win is that 4 KB-LDS/71-VGPR work-groups let the ~44 empty (early-return) WGs per row tile drain faster, not higher occupancy of real work. It is a genuine win against production as deployed (bound grid), but it overlaps heavily with the same engineer's wl_cap idea; on top of a capped/looped grid the marginal value is the engineer's 4-5% (cap 8 still has 50% empties at E=4) and would be ~1.5% with a truly exact grid. Practical size: -30 us/layer at b=8 (1.2 ms/step), -5 us at b=4 (0.2 ms/step, matches est_ms_per_token 0.2), 0 at b<=2; production decode runs mostly 1-4 rows, so this is a <0.5% step-time item on its own.
  - merge notes: 1) Add a second symbol in crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/mxfp4_pair_matvec.hip compiled with MXFP4_KW_MAX_CHUNK=8 (e.g. mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_kwide_c8; simplest is a second TU or a templated body, since the macro is file-scope). 2) Wrapper (crates/v4flash-kernels/src/mxfp4_pair.rs launch_fused_swiglu_kwide_ex): select the c8 symbol when b <= 8 AND chunk_size <= 8; add a hard assert chunk_size <= 8 for that symbol (the existing check is only against MXFP4_KW_MAX_CHUNK=32; passing 32 to the c8 kernel overflows s_member_packed[8]/s_q8v silently). 3) chunk_size must be 8 for the WHOLE chain at that b: moe_work_items_builder, kwide, and kwide2 share work_items, so remote_experts.rs batched_pass (CHUNK_SIZE const, RE:76, used at RE:4414/4438/4484 and the second hits-first pass) and forward_prefill.rs (CHUNK_SIZE at FP:8523, used for builders at 8735/8811, bound at 8821-8825, gate/up 8893 and down 9046-9117) need a per-call value chosen by b; prefill (b=512/1024, n_wi 300+) must keep 32 and the 32-chunk symbol. 4) moe_wi_upper_bound(6b, G, 8, cap) equals the chunk-32 bound for b <= 8 when G >= 6b; when the hub's sparse pool bound is smaller than 6b it grows by ceil(6b/8)-ceil(6b/32) <= 4 extra WGs (harmless; verify work_items capacity, which is bounded by members = 6b anyway). 5) Groups can exceed 8 members only if one row carries the same expert twice (miss-substitution paths); the chunked kernel then splits the group into two work items and remains correct, so no new invariant is needed, but the wrapper should not assume 'never splits'. 6) Test: bit-exact vs the 32-chunk symbol at b=1..8 including an 8-member group and a split group (>8 members) on the real box-2 selection path; keep the tail/odd-row cases. 7) Decide jointly with wl_cap: if the grid.y cap/exact grid lands, re-measure c8's marginal gain (1.5% with an exact grid) before paying for a second symbol.

#### `A_moe_smallb/gu2_lds_staged` — measured_neutral
- kernel: `mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_kwide (gate+up)`
