#### `A_moe_smallb/down_reduce_fusion` — untested
- kernel: `kwide2/dn2 + q2_k_reduce_partials_hetsplit + partials memset`
- idea: Accumulate the 6 slots of a row into out with float atomics instead of writing [b x 6 x 5120] partials and reducing: drops 1 memset + 1 kernel (8-12 us per layer) but makes the summation order run-to-run non-deterministic (fails the expert-cache determinism rule). The deterministic alternative (one WG owns all slots of a row) re-streams weights per row = the b=1 twin (2x at b=4). Priced, not pursued: the partials write + reduce cost 8-12 us of a 420-620 us chain (2%).

**Dead ends (A_moe_smallb — routed MoE at small batch (decode), iGPU gfx1151: mxfp4 kwide gate/up + kwide2 down chain (b=1..8) plus builders/q8k/reduce, with the b=1 hetsplit twins as reference):** (1) Dedicated small-b gate/up (gu_smallb v1: no LDS, predicated members, full unroll): 1.55-1.73x SLOWER - 192 VGPR + spills, and the uniform predicate branches turn every super-block pair into its own basic block so loads are issued and drained per pair. (2) gu2 fully-unrolled straight-line forms (D=5): the compiler hoists every invariant weight load (const __restrict__ args) to the kernel entry regardless of __builtin_amdgcn_sched_barrier, then spills 700-960 dwords (20x slower); pinning addresses through asm volatile only moves the spill to the 64-bit pointers. A rolled loop with a 2-pair ring is spill-free but then only neutral (mb4) or a loss (mb8: 41.6 KB LDS = 1 WG/CU). (3) gu3 (kwide + next-pair prefetch across the barriers): neutral - gate/up at small b is not MLP-limited with the hub idle (202/179 GB/s at b=2/4); the 04:00 ATT picture (63% of stall on one vmcnt) was contaminated by production traffic and must not be used to motivate a gate/up rewrite. (4) dn2 with 1 row/warp: 0.98/1.02/1.07 (loss at b>=4). (5) MXFP4_KW_UNROLL=8 and dn2 member-loop unroll 2: neutral / compiler refused. (6) 'exact grid = no gain' from 04:00 was wrong under load: with the GPU idle the 6b-bound empties cost 25+48 us at b=8 (now fixed by the work-item loop cap without a host readback). (7) chunk_size=8 alone on the production build (base.c8): nothing - the kwide_c8 gain is the smaller compile-time arrays (occupancy), not chunking. (8) Fusing q8_k_quantize into the gate/up epilogue: blocked by the grid geometry (a Q8_K block spans 32 WGs). (9) Down+reduce fusion via atomics: non-deterministic; deterministic variant re-streams weights; the reduce+memset are only 8-12 us anyway.

**Notes (A_moe_smallb — routed MoE at small batch (decode), iGPU gfx1151: mxfp4 kwide gate/up + kwide2 down chain (b=1..8) plus builders/q8k/reduce, with the b=1 hetsplit twins as reference):** Baseline drift: the 04:00 numbers in NOTES.md (chain 593/715/872 us at b=2/4/8, kwide 343 us) were taken with the production hub live; with the hub down the same harness gives 400/480/615 (chain) and 280 (kwide b=4). All ratios reported here are from interleaved A/Bs taken in the idle regime (each candidate vs the production chain in the same process), so they are internally consistent, but absolute production-time gains under hub load may differ. Harness accounting fix: at b=1 a row picks ppr=3 of the 4 pool slots, so only 3 experts (56.4 MB) are read; the harness now counts distinct experts actually read (E_eff), which also corrects the twins (row 0's picks: 3 experts at every b; the earlier '198/236 GB/s' twin figures were 4/3 overstated, corrected 187/209 = the CLOSED numbers). The harness matches production geometry/args (grid (288|320, min(6b, G+ceil(6b/32))) x 256, devcount pointer, chunk 32, remap/sentinel semantics, graph mode); differences: synthetic data, a 384-slot gbound standing in for the pool-slot count, and box 2's non-graph direct launches (per-launch overhead not measured). No new profiling this session: iGPU ATT is refused by the scheduler and the 04:00 ATT (results/att_kwide*_b4.txt) was under load; the bottleneck statements come from ISA (isa_all.sh: VGPR/LDS/spills/movrel), the exact-vs-bound grid parts A/B, and the candidate ablations. est_ms_per_token is reported as ms per decode STEP at the given row count (every step advances each stream by one token): b=4 one lane 2.5 ms, b=8 two lanes 5.3 ms, E=6 regime 4.4/7.3 ms; 40 layers x per-layer chain delta. All GPU jobs of this family have completed (gpu_queue shows 0 pending); WINS.log lines appended at 20:45 UTC. #### `C2_dense_prefill/engram_i8x_db` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `q8_0_gemm_wmma_lds_tiled -> q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x_db`
- idea: Engram wkv GEMM with the f16x 128x128x32 tile structure (perm-trick Q8_0 dequant, b128 LDS fragments), int8+xscale activations dequantised at stage exactly as lds_tiled does ((f16)((float)q*xs)), double-buffered LDS (40 KB, 1 barrier per k-outer, fragment ds_loads issued before the next tile's stage) and a 2-deep register prefetch. Same signature and inputs as gemm_lds_tiled; grid (ceil(b/128), M/128, 1) x 256. Per 64-row chunk 905 -> 484 us. Leverage: 8 chunks x 2 layers x 194 lanes = 3104 calls per 100K prefill -> 2.8 s -> 1.5 s of dGPU time = 0.83% of the 160 s wall as an upper bound (dGPU is overlapped with box-2 MoE, so the wall effect today is ~0; it pays when box 2 stops being the pole). Not a decode kernel.
- engineer: 905.00 -> 484.00 us, x1.860, runs=6
- correctness: bit_exact=True; bit-exact vs q8_0_gemm_wmma_lds_tiled on identical inputs at b=64, 128, 1, 17, 63 (16/16 CMP rows, results/corr_final_engram.txt; also every earlier run)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.00, est_prefill_pct=0.83; integration_cost=small
- files: cand.hip (q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x_db, f16x_core<128,128,2,4,2,BI8=true,LB,0,BRF,DB=true>), harness.cpp, run_engram.sh, results/engram_r5..r7/, results/w0_r1/, results/corr_final_engram.txt
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/C2_dense_prefill && bash build_base.sh && bash build.sh && bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 700 --label C2_dense_prefill/engram -- ./harness_gfx1201 gfx1201 . engram --m 25600 --corr --cand q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x_db`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.866 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True

Repro (3 separate scheduler jobs, label review/C2_dense_prefill, cold 96 MB flush, 60 interleaved rounds): base 905.8 / 902.4 / 901.2 us, cand 484.3 / 483.7 / 482.9 us -> median speedup 1.866 (engineer: 1.86). b=128: base 2247 (bimodal, p10 1663) -> cand 512 (matches engineer). Correctness: 17/17 CMP rows bit-exact, nonfinite=0: engineer's shapes (b=64,128,1,17,63) plus my untested ones b=9 (smallest non-dp4a production chunk), 33, 65 (2 baseline N-tiles), 100, 129 (2 candidate N-tiles), 200; and a second harness (harness_dn) with log-uniform weight/activation scales down to 2^-22 / 2^-24 including exact zeros, so q*scale exercises the f16 denormal/underflow range the engineer's [0.002,0.02] inputs never reach -- still bit-exact (the perm-trick dequant is a single f16 rounding of an exactly-representable product, same as (f16)((float)q*scale); k-order and WMMA op are identical). Traps checked: no warm-cache flattery (weight > MALL, flush on top); no extra kernel needed (same xq/xscale inputs, same f32 [b,M] output, all rows written -- out2 memset+compare would catch skipped outputs); inputs random not degenerate; leverage arithmetic honest (195 lanes x 2 layers x 8 chunks = 3120 calls, 2.8 s -> 1.5 s of dGPU time, engineer states wall effect ~0 while box 2 is the pole; est_ms_per_token=0 correct, not a decode kernel). Candidate: 166 VGPR, 40960 B LDS (1 WG/CU), no scratch/spills. Minor: the engineer's cand.hip/harness.cpp were touched at 19:48 after the r5-r7 results (19:35), but the current source builds the identical hsaco and reproduces the numbers, so nothing changed in this kernel.
  - merge notes: Integration = add q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x_db (f16x_core<128,128,2,4,2,BI8=true,LB,0,BRF,DB=true> plus its helpers q8x_magic4 and sync_lds) to crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/q8_0_matvec_wmma.hip, a Rust wrapper next to gemm_lds_tiled (q8_0.rs:614) with grid (ceil(b/128), M/128, 1) x 256 and args (out, w, xq, xscale, K, M, n_groups=1, batch, blocks), and switch the FP:3526 else-branch to it. New preconditions the wrapper MUST assert (lds_tiled only needed M%64, K%32): M % 128 == 0 (25600 ok) and K % 256 == 0 (b128 loads of xq rows and of the Q8_0 row at offset blocks*34 + ko require blocks % 8 == 0; 6144 ok; also out rows M % 4 for the float4 stores). Keep the small_b_dense_dp4a(n) <= 8 gate and the V41_ENGRAM_GEMV rollback in front of it; ldx is K (dense xq), no pitch. The kernel needs 40 KB static LDS and 256 threads (__launch_bounds__(256)); the gfx1151 build must keep the non-gfx12 stub pattern of the file. Per the engineer's own module-composition finding, re-measure the exact shipped file (4-kernel module) with the standalone harness before deploying, then run the project's prefill fidelity gate only as a smoke test (bit-exact, so KLD gate not required by the rule). The 128-row-chunk follow-up (512 us per 128 rows vs 2x484) is a separate change (+13 MB engram_kv/xq scratch, FP:3526 loop step 128) and is not part of this claim. Expected effect: ~1.3 s less dGPU time per 100K prefill (0.8% upper bound), ~0 wall today because dGPU overlaps box-2 MoE.

#### `C2_dense_prefill/engram_chunk128` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x_db at 128-row chunks`
- idea: Run the Engram wkv GEMM on 128-row chunks instead of 64 (halves the 167 MB weight re-reads): kernel-level 2 x 484 us (two 64-row chunks) -> 512 us for one 128-row chunk, i.e. 96% of the per-chunk DRAM roof (2 x 261 us). Integration: the chunk loop at FP:3526 steps 128 and needs ~13 MB more kv scratch. Combined with i8x_db: 2.8 s -> 0.8 s of dGPU time per 100K prefill = 1.3% upper bound.
- engineer: 968.00 -> 512.00 us, x1.890, runs=5
- correctness: bit_exact=True; bit-exact vs lds_tiled at b=128 (and 1/17/63) in every run (results/corr_final_engram.txt)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.00, est_prefill_pct=1.30; integration_cost=medium
- files: cand.hip (same kernel), run_engram.sh (--b 128), results/engram_r5..r7/engram_m25600_b128.txt
- repro: `bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 700 --label C2_dense_prefill/engram -- ./harness_gfx1201 gfx1201 . engram --m 25600 --b 128 --corr --cand q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x_db`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.893 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Reproduced exactly: 3 separate scheduler jobs (review/C2_dense_prefill), engineer's repro command, my own rebuild in review/engram_chunk128/ (baseline hsaco byte-identical to the in-tree q8_0_matvec_wmma.hip at $KFLAGS_V41; i8x_db ISA identical to the engineer's cand hsaco). b=64: 483.9/484.5/483.5 us; b=128: 511.9/511.2/508.1 us -> 2x483.9/511.2 = 1.89 (claim 1.89). Production lds_tiled measured alongside: 903/902/901 us per 64-row chunk (grid (400,ceil(b/64))x128, args (out,w,xq,xs,K,M,b,blocks) = q8_0.rs:644-655; cold: 96 MB flush > 64 MB MALL + 8 MB L2, and the 167 MB weight exceeds the MALL anyway, matching the production chunk loop where no weight survives between chunks; prefill is direct-launch, not graph-captured; in-tree crates unmodified vs 361d4f9). Correctness: 48/48 CMP rows bit-exact vs production lds_tiled on random int8/scale/weight inputs (not degenerate), at b = 1, 17, 63, 64, 65, 100, 127, 128, 129, 256, 300, 512 (the engineer had only 1/17/63/64/128); the kernel writes every (n<batch, m<M) output, masks out-of-range tokens with a clamped-row load + select, and the per-(row,m) result does not depend on chunk position, so chunk 128 vs 64 gives identical bits. Caveats (none refuting): (1) the claim's baseline_med_us=968 is the CANDIDATE kernel at two 64-row chunks, not production; vs production (2x902=1804 per 128 rows) the combined win is 3.5x, so the JSON baseline is conservative, not flattering. (2) '96% of the per-chunk DRAM roof (2x261)' uses the wrong denominator: a 128-row chunk streams the weight ONCE, so its own roof is 261 us and 511 us is 51% of it; the kernel is not DRAM-bound (b=64 costs 484, b=128 costs 511: it is per-WG chain bound at 1 WG/WGP with 40 KB LDS). (3) est_prefill_pct=1.3% is i8x_db + chunk128 combined (8x2x194 chunks: 905 -> 484 -> 4 chunks at 511: 2.81 s -> 1.50 s -> 0.79 s per 100K); the chunk128 increment alone is ~0.7 s = ~0.4%, and it is dGPU time, not wall time (box 2 paging is the prefill pole). (4) scratch: engram_kv/xq/xscale are sized by ENGRAM_CHUNK (batch_scratch.rs:1212-1214), so 128 rows = 13.1 MB total kv = +6.6 MB more, not '+13 MB'. (5) The lever does not stop at 128: b=256 = 824 us (0.81x of 2x511), b=512 = 1580 us (0.77x of 4x511), both bit-exact; larger chunks cost +19.7 MB / +45.9 MB more scratch. Raw outputs: review/engram_chunk128/results/t_r{1,2,3}_b{64,128}.txt and s_b{65,100,127,129,256,300,512}.txt.
  - merge notes: Integration is a config change on top of the engram_i8x_db integration (this claim has no value without it): (a) config.rs ENGRAM_CHUNK 64 -> 128 (or 256/512 if VRAM allows; hub takes ~15.5 GB of the 16 GB dGPU, so check free VRAM after the +6.6 MB per BatchScratch instance, times the number of lanes/scratch sets); batch_scratch.rs engram_xq/xscale/kv follow ENGRAM_CHUNK automatically. (b) Add q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x_db to kernels/q8_0_matvec_wmma.hip (module goes 4 -> 5 kernels; NOTES flag a once-seen module-composition timing anomaly, since refuted, but re-measure the exact file that ships, in that module) and a q8_0.rs wrapper mirroring gemm_lds_tiled with checks k%32==0, n_rows%128==0 (25600 ok; lds_tiled only required %64), batch>0, weight byte length, grid (ceil(b/128), M/128, 1) x 256, args (out, w, xq, xs, K, M, n_groups=1, batch, blocks) -- note the extra n_groups argument vs lds_tiled; xq row stride = K and xscale row stride = K/32, as production engram_xq/engram_xscale already are. (c) forward_prefill.rs:3526 route to the new wrapper; keep the small_b_dense_dp4a(n) GEMV arm for chunk tails <= 8 rows and the V41_ENGRAM_GEMV rollback; add a V41_ENGRAM_I8X=0 style rollback to lds_tiled. (d) The decode twin (forward_layer.rs) is untouched. (e) Tests: bit-exact vs lds_tiled at n in {9, 17, 63, 64, 65, 100, 127, 128} plus an end-to-end assertion that a 512-row lane (4 chunks) and a 300-row lane (128,128,44) produce bit-identical engram_kv/residual to the current 64-row chunking (true by construction, cheap to assert); no KLD gate needed since the kernel is bit-exact vs production. (f) Report the gain as dGPU time (0.7 s per 100K for chunk128 alone, 2.0 s combined) and verify on the real workload whether any wall-clock TTFT moves, since box-2 paging is the prefill critical path.

#### `C2_dense_prefill/replay_bpack_z16` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `q8_0_gemv_batched_warp8 / q8_0_grouped_gemv_batched -> q8_0_gemv_bpack_z16 / q8_0_grouped_gemv_bpack_z16`
- idea: The production bpack16 kernel body (GEMV_BPACK_MAX=16, same bl striding, same ws*xscale*dot expression and warp_sum tree) with the batch split into 16-row chunks on grid.z = ceil(b/16); each z-slice offsets xq/xscale/out by 16 rows. Weight re-read 4x at b=64 instead of 64x, and 4x the WGs. Replay per lane-layer (qb+wob+woa+kv): 4.27 ms -> 1.71 ms; x40 lane-layers (2 lanes x 20 decoder layers) = -0.10 s per 100K request = 0.064% of 160 s (replay is box-2 paging bound). Decode is unaffected (bpack16 already covers b<=16). Integration: one kernel per file + grid.z in matvec_bpack/matvec_grouped_bpack and drop the cap check (bpack_ok) for the replay path.
- engineer: 1519.00 -> 585.00 us, x2.600, runs=4
- correctness: bit_exact=True; bit-identical per (row,b) by construction and measured: 48/48 CMP rows bit-exact at b=64,1,5,17,33,63 on qb64/kv64/wob64/woa64 (results/corr_final_replay.txt)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.00, est_prefill_pct=0.06; integration_cost=small
- files: cand.hip (q8_0_gemv_bpack_z16, q8_0_grouped_gemv_bpack_z16), harness.cpp, run_replay.sh, results/replay_r1..r3/, results/corr_final_replay.txt
- repro: `bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 500 --label C2_dense_prefill/replay -- bash run_replay.sh rN --corr`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x2.620 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: None disqualifying. Baseline fidelity: in-tree q8_0_matvec.hip / q8_0_grouped_matvec.hip / q8_0.rs / forward_prefill.rs are unmodified vs 361d4f9; I rebuilt the baseline hsacos from those sources with the exact $KFLAGS_V41 --genco --offload-arch=gfx1201; the harness launches the same symbols (q8_0_gemv_batched_warp8, q8_0_grouped_gemv_batched) with the same grid (ceil(M/8),1,b)x256 and argument lists as q8_0.rs:261/869. Production regime verified from code: the replay runs once per prefill job (prefill_job_finish) with b_seg=min(t,SWA_WINDOW=128), lane_split cuts 64/64 with no image spans, prefill_f32_matvec(64) routes to the dp4a arm, bpack_ok(64) is false -> grid.z=64 kernel, direct launches (prefill is not graph-captured), weights read once per lane-layer (cold; harness flushes 96 MB > MALL+L2). Re-run (3 separate scheduler jobs, medians): qb 1508->576 us (2.62x), wo_b 1498->537 (2.79x), wo_a 1210->535 (2.26x), kv 52->47 (1.09x); lane-layer sum 4268->1695 us = -2.57 ms x 40 lane-layers (2 lanes x 20 decoder layers, CED_DECODER_START=20) = -0.10 s dGPU time per request, matching the claim. Correctness: 24/24 CMP rows bit-exact in my --corr run plus 60/60 on shapes the engineer did not test (b=1, 7, 49 = 3 slices + 1-row tail, 128 = whole replay window, and tails 5/17/33/63) on all four sites; numerics identical by construction (same dp4a chain order, same ws*xscale*dot expression, same lane striding and warp_sum tree). Traps checked: cold regime honest; same flags; no skipped outputs or new alignment assumptions; single kernel each side (no hidden reduce/repack); random non-degenerate inputs; call count verified; candidate 51 VGPR, 0 scratch, no LDS (the 20480 B in the isa.sh table is the known off-by-one row; disassembly has only warp_sum's ds_bpermute). Caveats: (1) device time only: the replay is box-2 paging-bound and the two lanes' dGPU kernels may overlap, so -0.10 s/request (0.064%) is an upper bound on wall time; (2) measured from the 40-kernel cand module, and the engineer documented a 1.6x module-composition effect on another kernel, so re-measure the exact kernel in the shipped module; (3) kv gain (9%) is small and consistent across 6 processes but not what the claim rests on; kv at b=7 is 1.4x slower than the grid.z kernel, which is irrelevant because production already uses bpack16 (= z16 at grid.z=1) below 17 rows.
  - merge notes: Add the z-chunked kernels to q8_0_matvec.hip and q8_0_grouped_matvec.hip (or add the blockIdx.z*16 row offset to the existing q8_0_gemv_bpack_warp8 / q8_0_grouped_gemv_bpack bodies: at grid.z=1 decode's path is unchanged). In crates/v4flash-kernels/src/q8_0.rs: matvec_bpack and matvec_grouped_bpack launch grid (grid_x, 1, batch.div_ceil(16)); drop or raise the batch > GEMV_BPACK_MAX error; bpack_ok keeps the V41_GEMV_BPACK=0 rollback but stops capping at 16 (or caps at 64 = the dp4a arm's threshold). Keep a separate 16-row cap for the head: het/scratch.rs:38 sizes head scratch on GEMV_BPACK_MAX and forward_head.rs:94 calls matvec_bpack, so lifting the kernel cap must not hand the head b>16 with a 16-row scratch. Tests: bit-exact vs q8_0_gemv_batched_warp8 / q8_0_grouped_gemv_batched at b=17..64 incl. tails 49/63 and b=128 (the review harness in review/replay_bpack_z16 does this); decode b<=16 must remain byte-identical in behaviour (same kernel, grid.z=1); re-measure the kernel in the shipped module (module-composition effect); check prefill_job_replay elapsed_s before/after on a >128-token prompt and expect <=0.1 s, possibly ~0 wall-clock because replay is box-2 paging bound. Rollback: V41_GEMV_BPACK=0 restores the grid.z kernel.

#### `C2_dense_prefill/f16x_db_bn64` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x -> q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x_db_bn64 (kv, q_a)`
- idea: 128x64 tile (8 waves as 4x2, wave tile 32x32), double-buffered LDS (30 KB -> 2 WGs/WGP), one LDS-only barrier per k-outer, 2-deep register prefetch with the select applied at stage time. Doubles the WG count of the occupancy-starved projections: kv 106 -> 59 us (1.79x), q_a 125 -> 97 us (1.28x) at b=512; at b=256 kv 0.54, q_a 0.65. Loses 9-25% on qb/wo_a/wo_b (per-WG intensity halves) so it must be selected per site (M<=1280). Lane-layer saving 47+28 = 75 us x 3880 = 0.29 s of dGPU time per 100K prefill (0.18% upper bound).
- engineer: 106.10 -> 59.10 us, x1.790, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; bit-exact on all 7 production shapes x b in {512,1,3,17,100,129,255,256,500} (results/corr_final_f16x.txt 378/378)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.00, est_prefill_pct=0.18; integration_cost=medium
- files: cand.hip (F16X_DB(q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x_db_bn64,128,64,4,2,2)), harness.cpp (grid (ceil(b/64), M/128, G) x 256), run_ab_f16x.sh, results/ab_f16x_r5..r7/kv_b512.txt, qa_b512.txt
- repro: `bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 500 --label C2_dense_prefill/ab -- bash run_ab_f16x.sh rN q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x_db_bn64`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.780 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Baseline fidelity: rebuilt the UNMODIFIED in-tree crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/q8_0_matvec_wmma.hip with exactly build.rs's flags (-O3 --genco -DDEEPSTRIX_V41=1 -DMHC_N_EMBD=5120 -DMHC_HC_DIM=20480 -DROUTER_MAX_EXPERTS=512, gfx1201); my base hsaco is byte-identical to the engineer's. Harness launches the same symbol q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x with the wrapper's grid (ceil(b/128), M/128, G) x 256 and the same 9 args (q8_0.rs:604); shapes match the call sites (kv FP:3990 M=512 K=5120 ldx=f16_pitch(5120)=5184; q_a dispatch.rs:305 via dense_gemm_prefill M=1280 K=5120 ldx=5184). Prefill dense GEMMs are direct launches on de.compute (the graph capture at FP:1033 is the decode arena StageCap), so direct-launch timing is the right regime. Cold weights via a 96 MB memset flush (> 64 MB MALL + 8 MB L2) between every timed call; note the flush also evicts the activations, which in production were just written by f32_to_f16_cast_2d, so the true production point lies between my cold and warm numbers. Repro (my own build in review/f16x_db_bn64/, 3 separate processes via the scheduler, label review/C2_dense_prefill, 60 interleaved rounds each): kv b=512 base 105.20/107.12/105.24 us -> cand 59.20/59.04/59.20 (ratio 0.563/0.551/0.563, median speedup 1.78x vs claimed 1.79x); q_a b=512 base 124.36/124.32/124.56 -> cand 96.56/96.20/96.72 (0.776/0.774/0.777, 1.29x vs claimed 1.28x); kv b=256 0.546/0.545/0.551 and q_a b=256 0.653/0.656/0.656 match the claimed 0.54/0.65; p10s consistent. Warm-cache control (no flush, inner=5): kv 86.7 -> 38.8 (0.447), q_a 101.4 -> 88.8 (0.876): the win is not a flush artefact, but q_a's gain shrinks from 22% cold to 12% warm, so if the 7 MB q_a weight is MALL-resident when the second lane runs the same layer the realised q_a gain is nearer 12-15%. Correctness: 181/181 CMP rows bit-exact (bit_diff=0, nonfinite=0) on all 7 production shapes including wo_a's 8 groups, at b in {1,3,7,17,63,64,65,100,129,191,255,256,500,511,512} and additionally {513,1000,1023,1024} for kv/qa/shg/shd; the harness zeroes the candidate output before each launch and compares the full b*G*M outputs, so skipped writes would fail. Inputs are random weights, random f16 scales in [0.002,0.02] and random f16 activations in [-1,1] (not degenerate). Bit-exactness is by construction: identical dequant expression, identical per-accumulator k order (k_outer ascending, two 16-wide inner steps), identical WMMA; the tile/wave remap only changes which lane owns which output. Traps checked: no separate reduce/repack kernel (same signature, same output layout); no alignment beyond production's (16 B loads at offsets production also uses; x_pitch % 8 is enforced by the wrapper); the branch-free B load clamps out-of-range tokens to row batch-1 (always mapped, batch>0 guaranteed by the wrapper) and masks at stage time; candidate resources 107 VGPRs, 30720 B LDS, 0 scratch/spills (2 WGs/WGP as claimed). Caveat that matters for integration: q_a at b=1024 LOSES (my run: 165.0 -> 171.7 us, ratio 1.040; engineer's r5-r7 agree 1.044), while kv at b=1024 still wins (109.2 -> 78.4, 0.718). The claim's own 'select per site (M<=1280)' rule is therefore incomplete: q_a must additionally be gated on b<=512. The engineer's other data also shows db_bn64 losing 9-25% on qb/wo_a/wo_b/shared at 512 rows, so the rule must not widen. Magnitude: 47+28 = 75 us per lane-layer x 3880 lane-layers = 0.29 s of dGPU time per 100K prefill, arithmetic checks out, but it is a dGPU-time bound: the family's own notes and PREFILL_100K say the dGPU is not the prefill pole (box-2 paging is), so the end-to-end effect is expected to be near zero; est_prefill_pct 0.18 is honestly labelled an upper bound.
  - merge notes: Integration = add q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x_db_bn64 (the F16X_DB(128,64,4,2,2) instantiation from scratch/kernel_ideas/C2_dense_prefill/cand.hip; it needs the f16x_core template, sync_lds fence pair and q8x_magic4 helper, ~350 lines, or a hand-flattened copy) to crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/q8_0_matvec_wmma.hip, and a wrapper twin of gemm_f16x in crates/v4flash-kernels/src/q8_0.rs (same 9 args, same checks m%128, k%32, x_pitch>=n_groups*k and %8) that launches grid (batch.div_ceil(64), m/128, n_groups) x 256. Selection: route only the kv site (FP:3990, M=512) and the q_a site (dispatch.rs:305 / FP:3836, M=1280) to it, and only for b<=512 at q_a (kv wins at 256/512/1024; q_a loses 4% at 1024 and the shared expert M=2304 loses at 512) -- express it as a small-M/small-b predicate with an env rollback (V41_F16X_BN64=0), not as a blanket replacement of gemm_f16x. Confirm the production lane row count (the engineer assumes 512 rows per lane; if any prefill lane runs 1024 rows q_a must stay on the production kernel there). Tests to add: extend crates/v4flash-kernels/tests/q8_0_gemm_f16x.rs so the CPU-oracle test and a bit-exact f16x-vs-bn64 comparison run on M in {512,1280} at b in {1,7,63,64,65,255,256,500,511,512,513,1024} (tails around the 64-row tile are the new edge). Re-measure the shipped file in the 4-kernel production module with bench_v41_kernel_roofline prefill_dgpu (kv/q_a rows) after relinking, since the number was measured in a 40-kernel scratch module (the engineer's modtest says module size does not matter, but the r1 i8x 1.6x anomaly in the same family is unexplained). Bit-exact, so no KLD fidelity gate is needed; a whole-step prefill A/B is still worth one run only to confirm no regression, since the expected end-to-end gain is within noise (dGPU is not the prefill pole).

(1) Baseline fidelity: I rebuilt the baseline myself from the unmodified in-tree crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/q8_0_matvec_wmma.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco --offload-arch=gfx1201 (= build.rs: -O3 --genco + the four -D defines, no extras); md5 9b79c6e1... is byte-identical to the engineer's base hsaco, and the candidate hsaco (87ab7e52...) likewise. Harness launch_base uses the same symbol (q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x), same grid (ceil(b/128), M/128, n_groups) x 256, same 9-arg list and types as q8_0.rs:603-612; shapes/ldx = f16_pitch(dim)=dim+64 match FP:3887 (qb) and FP:6129 (wo_a, n_groups=8). Regime: cold weights via 96 MB flush (> 64 MB MALL + 8 MB L2), direct launch; prefill f16x is not graph-captured (ms_graphs is the decode arena only), so direct launch is correct. inner=1 (one call per flushed block). (2) Reproduction, 3 separate processes via the scheduler (--label review/C2_dense_prefill), 60 interleaved rounds each: qb b=512 base med 447.9/449.6/449.7 us -> cand 403.9/403.6/403.8 us = 1.113x (p10 442-444 vs 398-399); wo_a b=512 base 362.8/361.8/362.8 -> cand 322.7/322.7/322.5 = 1.124x. Claim (1.11 / 1.13) reproduces within 1%. Extra shape not in the claim: b=1024 (max production chunk) qb 1027 -> 814 us (1.26x), wo_a 645 -> 592 us (1.09x). (3) Correctness: 91/91 CMP rows bit-exact (bit_diff=0) against the production kernel on random non-degenerate inputs (random Q8 bytes with f16 scales in [0.002,0.02], random f16 activations in [-1,1]) on all seven f16x sites at b=1024 plus tails 1,3,17,100,129,255,256,500 and the engineer-untested odd batches 7, 257, 513, 1023. No re-association: same WMMA f16->f32 accumulation order per output, only the tile/wave geometry changes. (4) Traps checked: candidate writes every output (out2 is zeroed before each candidate run, so missing stores would show as diffs); out-of-range tokens are clamped for the load and zeroed at stage, epilogue guards n_idx>=batch; alignment needs (x_pitch%8, K%32) equal the wrapper's existing guards; only new precondition is M%256==0 (true at every site: 32768, 1024/group, 512, 1280, 2304, 5120). ISA: 216 VGPR, 30 KB LDS, 0 scratch, 0 spills. The LDS-only barrier (sync_lds) is sound because the only cross-thread data is LDS; register consumers of the global prefetch get their own s_wait_loadcnt. No separate reduce/repack kernel is needed; the f32->f16 cast is shared by baseline and candidate. est arithmetic: 90 us x 3880 lane-layers = 0.35 s of dGPU time per 100K, stated as an upper bound; per memory the dGPU is not the prefill pole, so end-to-end gain may be ~0 -- the claim already says so.

Tests: extend tests/q8_0_gemm_f16x.rs oracle with the new symbol at qb/wo_a shapes incl. tails (b=1,7,129,257,513,1023,1024) and an m%256 != 0 rejection case; re-run bench_v41_kernel_roofline prefill_dgpu to confirm the 40-50 us/site delta in-tree; since it is bit-exact no KLD gate is required, but a one-shot prefill determinism check vs the current build is cheap insurance. #### `C2_dense_prefill/f16x_pf2` — measured_win

- idea: Production tile with an LDS-only barrier (release/acquire fence on the local address space + s_barrier: s_wait_dscnt instead of s_wait_loadcnt_dscnt 0), a 2-deep register prefetch, unconditional (clamped-row) B loads with the zero-select applied at stage time. wo_b 427 -> 411 (1.04x), shared gate/up 166 -> 148 (1.12x); the 128x128 double-buffered `db` variant gives shared down 136 -> 127 (1.07x). Together with db_bn64 and 256x128 the per-shape best set brings the f16x lane-layer sum from 1993 to 1723 us (-13.5%, 50% of the 853 us roof): -1.05 s of dGPU time per 100K prefill = 0.66% upper bound (dGPU not the pole).

#### `C2_dense_prefill/replay_bpack64` — measured_loss
- kernel: `q8_0_gemv_bpack64_warp8 / q8_0_grouped_gemv_bpack64`
- idea: Full b-packed dp4a GEMV for b<=64 (weight read once, 64 accumulators in registers, 102 VGPRs, no scratch after replacing the predicated break with a predicate). Bit-exact and 1.5x faster than the grid.z=64 baseline (qb64 1519 -> 1008, wob64 1493 -> 980, woa64 1204 -> 672) but 1.7x SLOWER than bpack_z16 and 3.6x slower on kv64: the per-lane 64-row activation loop has too little memory parallelism; 4 re-reads with 4x the WGs win. Do not adopt.

#### `C2_dense_prefill/i8x_r1_codegen` — measured_win
- kernel: `q8_0_gemm_wmma_i8x from cand_r1.hip (commit aab5a67)`
- idea: The first attempt's single-buffered i8x (production tile, int8 acts) is 905 -> 626 us (1.45x, bit-exact, 6 runs) when built from cand_r1.hip, but the same algorithm re-expressed in the current template runs at 983-1100 us. Module composition (8 vs 40 kernels: identical), LDS size (20480 both; the isa.sh LDS column is off by one row), VGPRs (148 vs 139, both under the 256 occupancy allows), the load-select placement, runtime vs compile-time A predicate, and an explicit wait before the WMMAs (W0: refuted, costs 3-25%) were all ruled out. Superseded by i8x_db (484 us); kept as a warning that this kernel's speed is codegen-sensitive: re-measure the exact file that ships.
- engineer: 905.00 -> 626.00 us, x1.450, runs=6

#### `C2_dense_prefill/splitk_wob` — untested
- kernel: `q8_0_gemm_wmma_f16x (wo_b 5120x8192)`
- idea: wo_b launches 160 WGs = 1.67 rounds of the 96 WG slots; a 2-way split-K would fill the machine but needs an f32 reduction (atomics are non-deterministic; a second pass costs 2 x 10 MB). Not attempted.
- correctness: bit_exact=False; a deterministic two-pass reduction would be bit-exact only if the k-order of the accumulation is preserved (it is not: two partial sums are added), so it needs the fidelity gate

- files: NOTES.md (idea 8)
- repro: `n/a`

**Dead ends (C2_dense_prefill):** (1) LDS-only barrier alone (lb) with production-style guarded loads: LOSS up to +21% (qa) — the ATT diagnosis 's_syncthreads waits on the prefetch' was right but fixing only the barrier just moved the wait. It becomes a 21% WIN on kv once the loads are unconditional and the zero-select is applied at stage time. (2) THE LOAD-SELECT TRAP: patch2 wrote `vb = b_on ? loaded : 0` right after the global load; the compiler materialised the select immediately and emitted s_wait_loadcnt right after the load, turning the prefetch into a synchronous load in every variant measured in r2-r4 (all those numbers were 5-25% pessimistic). Never touch a prefetched register before its use; do the masking after the barrier. (3) Prefetch depth > 2 (pf3/pf4): = pf2 within noise; ATT of pf3 still waits on the OLDEST stage (s_wait_loadcnt 0x3, 35% of stall) — the L2->CU tile-fetch path is throughput-bound (492 MB of tile traffic per q_b call at 1.1-1.3 TB/s), not latency-bound; ablations at q_b: no-global 0.72, no-LDS 0.82, no-WMMA 0.82 — the three phases serialize per WG (resource algebra: G=377 us largest, max(L,W)=330, real 458). (4) Double-buffered 128x128 at 1 WG/WGP (40 KB LDS) on q_b/wo_a/wo_b: 0.96-1.00, the occupancy loss cancels the pipelining; 128x64 single/double-buffered and 64x64 tiles on the big shapes: 1.09-1.42 slower (intensity halves). (5) 256x256 (512 thr) tiles: 1.05-1.8 slower everywhere; 256x128 double-buffered: 61 KB LDS + spills, 1.2-1.9. (6) bpack64 (weight read once, 64 accumulators): 1.5x over the grid.z=64 baseline but 1.7x slower than the 4-chunk z16 (and 3.6x slower on kv). (7) i8x_bn64 for Engram at b=64: 1.34-1.38 slower (grid.x=1 either way, half the WMMAs per barrier). (8) W0 (explicit s_wait_loadcnt 0 before the WMMA block, mimicking the fast r1 binary): 3-25% slower everywhere — loads floating across the WMMAs are good. (9) The r1 i8x 1.6x codegen gap: module size (8 vs 40 kernels), LDS size, VGPRs, select placement, A-predicate style and W0 all ruled out; unexplained, superseded by i8x_db.

**Notes (C2_dense_prefill):** Regime and fidelity of the baseline: harness = the unmodified in-tree .hip built with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco --offload-arch=gfx1201, launched with the exact Rust geometry and argument lists (f16x grid (ceil(b/128), M/128, G) x 256 with ldx = d+64; lds_tiled (M/64, ceil(b/64)) x 128; GEMV (M/8,1,b) x 256), synthetic Q8_0 weights with sane f16 scales, cold weights via a 96 MB memset between timed blocks (> 64 MB MALL + 8 MB L2), warm activations, direct launch (prefill stages are not graph-captured), 60 interleaved rounds, medians. Differences from production: synthetic data; kernels measured in isolation (no concurrent lanes); the production hub was DOWN for the whole resume so both GPUs were idle (the first attempt's numbers at 04:00 were taken under production load but agree within 3%). Engram was measured at the FULL M=25600 (the first attempt could only fit M=12800 with the hub up). Leverage caveat: the dGPU dense stages are ~54 s of a 160 s 100K prefill but overlapped with the box-2 MoE pole, so every est_prefill_pct above is an upper bound on dGPU time, not a wall-clock promise; replay savings are per request (0.10-0.14 s), not per decode token. Per-shape kernel selection is the practical form of the f16x win: db_bn64 for M<=1280 (kv, q_a), 256x128 for q_b/wo_a, pf2 or db for wo_b/shared; all bit-exact and re-verified on tails (b=1,3,17,100,129,255,256,500). Tooling notes for others: isa.sh's LDS column (and my kd_meta.sh) attribute each kernel's sizes to the PREVIOUS row because the .name key follows the sizes in the code-object metadata — read it shifted; isa.sh --dis truncates at 400 lines (use the full .s it writes next to the hsaco); the harness's stdout/stderr interleave can split a CMP line (separate stderr for correctness records). WINS.log has the live lines. Everything is under scratch/kernel_ideas/C2_dense_prefill/: NOTES.md (full log), cand.hip (all candidates; patch2..patch11 applied in order to the aab5a67 version), harness.cpp, run_*.sh, results/ (baseline_r1, ab_f16x_r1..r7, engram_r1..r7, replay_r1..r3, att_qb, att_kv, att_pf3_qb, corr_final_*.txt, engram_bisect, modtest_r1, modtest2, w0_r1). No GPU process of this family is left running or queued.

#### `D_attention/fused_vt_qreg_sp` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `attention_dec_score_htiled_wmma_f16s + attention_mixed_softmax_wsum_batched_htiled_wmma_ldsv_f16s (decode pair)`
- idea: ONE launch, grid (4, b) x 512, BIT-EXACT with the production pair: (1) score phase = production per-warp chain, but the 32 q fragments are cast to f16 ONCE per warp into 128 VGPRs so each tile issues only its 32 K loads (was 32 K + 64 q b128 loads + 256 cvt); scores stay in LDS; (2) production phase A, writing the f16 weights TILE-MAJOR (raw tail zero-padded) so the WMMA A fragment is one ds_load_b128; (3) phase B: each warp stages only its 2x16 columns of every 16-key V tile TRANSPOSED ([dim][key], 40 B row stride) into a private 2-slot LDS ring (8 ds_store_b32 per tile per lane after a register pack) so each B fragment is 2 x ds_load_b64 instead of 8 x ds_load_u16; 4 tiles of global loads in flight; stage of tile t+1 issued before the fragment loads of tile t; NO WG barrier after phase A. Measured (3 runs x 6 shapes, medians): b=1/2/3/4/5/8 = 19.9/19.9/20.0/20.4/20.6/21.8 us vs pair 41.9/41.1/43.8/46.9/47.4/53.3 (x0.48..x0.41). Arithmetic: at the typical b=3-4 per lane it saves 24-26.5 us per lane-layer; 40 layers per lane = ~1.0 ms of per-lane critical path, 80 lane-layers = ~2.0 ms/step of dGPU busy time (both lanes). Requires n_total <= 640, n_head % 16 == 0, head_dim 512, no comp_allowed_bits mask (decode passes none); does not write softmax weights back to sd.attn_scores (nothing reads them). 256 VGPRs, 62 KB LDS, 1 WG/WGP (fine: 4b WGs).
- engineer: 46.90 -> 20.40 us, x2.300, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; bit-exact vs the production pair on 7 shapes (b=1..8, gathered and dense comp_base_per stores, raw/comp partial tiles, n_comp=0, 1-key rows, an empty row); row 0 vs f64 CPU reference rel_rmse 2.133e-4 identical to production (results/check7_run1.txt, check6_run1.txt)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=2.00, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=small
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention/cand7_attn.hip (attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4; _d8 same), cand6_attn.hip (attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_d8, _vt_d8: the un-pipelined forms), attn_harness2.cpp, results/sp_run{1,2,3}.txt, results/final_run{1,2,3}.txt, results/check7_run1.txt, results/att_vtqreg_b8/
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention && bash build2.sh all && bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 96 --label D_attention/check -- env CANDS=attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4 ./attn_harness2_gfx1201 gfx1201 . check && bash final.sh sp 3 attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_sp_d8,attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4,attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_d8 (then bash w.sh TICKET results/sp_runN.txt; python3 agg.py r`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x2.310 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Reproduced and could not refute. (1) Baseline fidelity: I rebuilt base_attention_dec/base_attention_mixed from the UNMODIFIED in-tree .hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco into review/; both hsacos are byte-identical to the engineer's. The harness launches the same symbols (attention_dec_score_htiled_wmma_f16s, attention_mixed_softmax_wsum_batched_htiled_wmma_ldsv_f16s) with the same grid/block/args as crates/v4flash-kernels/src/attention_dec.rs:32 (grid (ceil(n/256), n_head/16, b) x 512, mask=null, max_keys_words=2568, stride 3072, kq_scale=1/sqrt(512), comp_kv_batch_stride=512 gathered / comp_base_per dense) and attention.rs:1117 (grid (4,b) x 512), matching the call sites forward_prefill.rs:5805/5889 (attn_dec_score_for(b) = b<=16, mask None). Graph mode = production decode regime. (2) Re-run: 3 separate scheduler runs of the exact repro (review label, my own binaries in review/vtqreg/): b=1/2/3/4/5/8 pair 41.40/41.38/43.57/46.90/47.20/53.29 us vs sp_d4 19.92/19.92/20.01/20.29/20.57/21.81 -> 2.08/2.08/2.18/2.31/2.29/2.44x; engineer claims 46.9->20.4 at b=4 (2.30x): within 1%. Ranges across runs <=2% except one 1.15x outlier round in run1. (3) Warm-cache trap checked: with a 256 MB flush before EVERY call (K/V, q, sinks all cold; direct launch, inner=1) the win holds at 1.90/1.95/1.73x (b=1: 51.7->27.2, b=4: 68.2->35.0, b=8: 83.9->48.5 us), i.e. the absolute saving per lane-layer is LARGER cold (24-35 us). (4) Correctness: bit-exact vs the production pair on the engineer's 7 shapes AND 7 shapes he did not test: b=16 (production max for the dec path), b=7 ragged with 1/15/17/511-key comp tails and n_raw 127/1, n_raw=0 comp-only rows + empty row + n_raw=5/n_comp=16, comp_kv=NULL n_comp=0 (ratio-0 layers L0-1), dense comp_base_per store at b=16 with mixed tails; outputs pre-filled with a NaN canary (no unwritten slots); repeated with a peaked softmax (q in [-8,8], weights underflowing to f16 zero) -> still bit-exact; f64 CPU reference rel_rmse identical to production (2.13e-4 at q0.5, 1.41e-3 at q8) on rows 0/2/4. ISA: sp_d4 256 VGPR, 0 scratch/spills, 62 KB LDS (sp_d8 has 1 spill, 8 B scratch; the claim's headline kernel is _d4). Caveats (not refutations): est_ms_per_token=2 is 80 lane-layer dGPU BUSY time; per-lane critical path is ~1.0 ms (~0.3% of a ~300 ms step, and the dGPU is often not the critical path - box-2 paging is); L0-1 score only 128 keys so their saving is smaller; the harness excludes the production event pair (4.6 us/stage) - fusing two stages into one would save one more pair, in the candidate's favour. The kernel has NO guard for n_total > 640 (WSTRIDE 664 LDS rows would overflow silently) and ignores comp_allowed_bits; both must be guarded by the wrapper.
  - merge notes: Integration = one new kernel (attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4 from scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention/cand7_attn.hip; gfx12-only, needs a gfx1151 stub or a wrapper arch check) + one Rust wrapper replacing the two launches at forward_prefill.rs:5805 (score) and :5889 (smwsum) inside the attn_dec_score_for(b) && !fused && !f32_scores branch, and the ratio-0 window-only branch at :5176/:5216 (comp_kv None, tested bit-exact). Wrapper MUST fall back to the existing pair when: eff_n_total_max > 640 (indexer-not-fired rows with n_comp > 512, or image rows whose raw window is up to 128+384 keys in a <=16-row tail chunk), comp_allowed_bits is Some (kernel has no mask path; decode passes None today), head_dim != 512 or n_head % 16 != 0, or V41_VERIFY_DECODE_ATTN=1 (the replay reads the softmax weights in sd.attn_scores, which the fused kernel no longer writes; nothing else reads them). Add a hard error/assert in the wrapper for n_total_max <= 640 rather than trusting the call site. Keep sd.attn_scores allocated (prefill and the b>16 path still use it). Collapse the two stage timers k.attn.score/k.attn.smwsum into one (saves an event pair); update evtrace/pftrace consumers that key on those stage names. Tests: extend tests/attention_dec_bitexact.rs (or a new one) to compare the fused output bit-exact against the pair on gathered + dense + NULL comp stores, ragged tails, b=1..16, n_raw=0 and empty rows; tests/multistream_row_bases.rs for the comp_base_per arena path; a bench_decode_latency_ab paired point at b=1/3/4 to confirm the ~25 us per lane-layer in situ with event pairs. Expected step-level effect is small (~1 ms per-lane critical path, ~2 ms dGPU busy at 2 lanes); do not expect a visible tok/s change above the ~8% E2E noise floor - verify with evtrace stage sums, not tok/s. The engineer's blk128 score candidate becomes moot if this is adopted. Raw outputs of my runs: scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention/review/vtqreg/{check_repro_run1,decode_repro_run1..3,check2_run1,check2_q8_run1,cold_run1}.txt; reviewer harness harness_review.cpp there.

#### `D_attention/score_blk128` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `attention_dec_score_htiled_wmma_f16s`
- idea: Same kernel body with 4 warps per WG instead of 16 (block 128, grid.x = ceil(n_total/64)): 4x more WGs spread the per-warp K/q load traffic over more CUs. Saves ~2-4 us per lane-layer at b<=4 -> x80 = ~0.2 ms/step. Only relevant if the fused kernel above is NOT adopted (it replaces this kernel).
- engineer: 15.40 -> 11.70 us, x1.320, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=True; bit-exact scores on all 7 check shapes (results/check2_run1.txt); attempt-1's 'bit_diff=40960' for blk256 was a harness artefact (compared against the weights-overwritten buffer)

- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.326 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Kernel-level claim reproduces and the baseline is faithful. Evidence: (1) Baseline fidelity: in-tree crates/v4flash-kernels/kernels/attention_dec.hip and src/attention_dec.rs are diff-identical to 361d4f9; my rebuild of the baseline hsaco with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco is md5-identical to the engineer's (7abd36b1...), and my rebuild of cand2_attn.hip is ISA-identical to the engineer's code object. The harness launches the production symbol attention_dec_score_htiled_wmma_f16s with grid (ceil(n_total_max/256), n_head/16=4, b) x 512, the same 14 args in the same order (mask=null as FP:5805 passes, max_keys_words=2568, n_head 64, scores_stride 3072, kq_scale 1/sqrt(512), comp_kv_batch_stride 512 with comp_base_per null = the gathered top-K path), graph mode with warm operands = the graph-captured decode stage. The blk128 body has the production instruction mix (histogram differs only by 52 s_nop + label), 212 VGPR / 0 LDS / 0 spills like production: same chain, same stores, no skipped work. (2) My 3 separate scheduler runs (review/final_run{1,2,3}.txt, medians of medians, ranges <=3% except one 1.17x outlier in run 1 that hit base and candidate alike): base dec_score 9.70/9.85/12.17/15.43/15.71/21.48 us vs blk128 6.81/8.44/9.94/11.64/15.02/20.10 at b=1/2/3/4/5/8 -> x1.42/1.17/1.22/1.33/1.05/1.07; engineer claimed 9.8->7.0 ... 15.4->11.7 (x1.32 at b=4): within 2%. blk256 = 6.85/9.68/9.94/12.72/15.29/18.54 (better than blk128 at b=8, as the engineer said). (3) Correctness: engineer's 7 check shapes re-run bit-exact; my extra harness (review/check_extra.cpp, 12 shapes the engineer did not test: b=16 = the attn_dec_score_for max, b=16 dense store, b=7 and b=13 ragged rows, n_total_max=129/145/161/192/9 so grid.x differs between 64- and 256-key blocks, no raw window, a random non-null comp_allowed_bits mask, an empty row inside b=16, a single 9-key partial tile) is bit-exact for blk256/blk128/blk64 with a 0x7C7C NaN sentinel proving every candidate writes exactly the slot count the production kernel writes (no dropped tails, no over-writes into the stride padding). Random non-degenerate inputs with 1/5 of the q rows scaled x40. Caveats, none of which refute the kernel number: (a) it is score-alone dGPU device time in a warm-L2 graph of 10 calls; in production each lane-layer's 640 KB of K comes from MALL/DRAM, which hurts the thin 12-48 WG baseline at least as much as the 48-192 WG candidate (more memory-level parallelism), so warmth does not flatter the candidate, but the absolute microseconds will differ; (b) est_ms_per_token 0.2 = 80 lane-layers x ~2.5 us of dGPU BUSY time; the per-lane critical path is 40 layers (~0.1-0.15 ms) and the two lanes' streams share the dGPU CUs, so a kernel that wins by occupying more CUs may win less when the other lane is running - unmeasured; either way it is <0.2% of a decode step; (c) blk128 loses 1.6 us to blk256 at b=8 and the two are equal at b=1 and b=3, so the '4 warps' choice only strictly wins at b=2 and b=4-5; b=6..16 timing was not measured by anyone (attn_dec_score_for allows b<=16); (d) the claim is moot if the fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4 kernel (which replaces this launch) is adopted.
  - merge notes: Drop-in change in crates/v4flash-kernels: (1) kernels/attention_dec.hip: SCD_KEYS_PER_BLK 256 -> 64 (=4 warps x 16 keys), __launch_bounds__(512) -> (128); nothing else in the body changes (n0 = blockIdx.x*SCD_KEYS_PER_BLK + warp*16 already generalises). (2) src/attention_dec.rs launch_score_f16s_rows: grid.x = n_total_max.div_ceil(64), block (128,1,1); keep the head_dim==512 / n_head%16 / n_total_max<=scores_stride checks. Better: make keys-per-block a batch-dependent choice - 64 keys/128 threads for b<=5, 128 keys/256 threads (blk256) for b>=6 (blk256 is 1.6 us faster at b=8 and equal at b=1/3); that means two kernel symbols (or a template) and a switch on `batch` in the wrapper. Tests: extend tests/attention_dec_bitexact.rs to the new block config(s) at b=1,4,8,16 with ragged n_raw/n_comp per row, n_total_max not a multiple of 64 and =1, the dense comp_base_per store, and a non-null comp_allowed_bits mask (the kernel keeps the masked path even though decode passes None); re-run tests/bench_decode_latency_ab.rs (paired) to confirm the -2..-4 us per lane-layer with event pairs and the real per-layer K cache regime, and measure once with both lanes live (V41_ATTN_DEC_SCORE path, b per lane 1-5) since the win comes from occupying more CUs that the other lane's stream may be using. gfx1151 build is unaffected (the body is #if gfx12-only, iGPU never launches it). Note the 0x7C7C-sentinel slot-count check in review/check_extra.cpp is a useful pattern for the in-tree bitexact test (the current harness memsets to 0, which cannot distinguish 'wrote 0' from 'did not write').

#### `D_attention/drop_f16_roundtrip` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `f16_roundtrip`
- idea: Delete the launch at FP:4038. Proof: after it the only readers of sd.kv_normed are kv_cache_append_batched (FP:4187, 4204) which store (_Float16)x (RN); f16(f32(f16(x))) == f16(x) for RN, so the f16 cache is bit-identical with or without it. (The FP8-window 'exact in f16' argument alone is NOT sufficient: products fall below 2^-24 when a 32-block's amax < 0.0068 and the roundtrip DOES change 20-40% of the f32 values in such rows; measured 529K changed f32 values, 0 changed cache bits.) Timing: kv chain fp8_act_quant + f16_roundtrip + kv_cache_append = 9.7/8.6/8.7 us at b=1/4/8 -> 7.3/6.4/6.5 without it: -2.2..-2.4 us per lane-layer x 80 = ~0.18 ms/step (graph node cost; also one fewer node in every prefill/replay chain, negligible there).
- engineer: 8.60 -> 6.40 us, x1.340, runs=1
- correctness: bit_exact=True; kv_cache_append output bit-identical with vs without f16_roundtrip over 10 magnitude sweeps x 262144 elements incl. blocks whose FP8 products are f16-subnormal (results/f16rt_run1.txt, f16rt_timing_run1.txt)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.18, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=drop-in
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention/attn_harness2.cpp (mode f16rt), results/f16rt_run1.txt, results/f16rt_timing_run1.txt
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention && bash build2.sh all && bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev dgpu --mb 96 --label D_attention/f16rt -- ./attn_harness2_gfx1201 gfx1201 . f16rt`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.347 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Baseline fidelity (verified against crates/v4flash-kernels): the three baseline code objects I rebuilt in review/ from the unmodified in-tree f16_roundtrip.hip / fp4_kv_quant.hip / kv_cache_append.hip with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco --offload-arch=gfx1201 are sha256-identical to the engineer's. Launch geometry matches the Rust wrappers exactly: F16Roundtrip::launch = grid ceil(n/256)x256 with n = b*512 (compressor.rs:590), launch_fp8_window = grid (b)x512 args (x, n_rows, width) (fp4_kv.rs:47), kv_cache_append_batched = grid (b)x512 args (cache, kv_new, n_raw_before, head_dim, slot_per) (kv_cache_append.rs:125). Regime: warm activations, graph mode; production's g.kv_chain stage IS graph-captured (FP stage_cap, f16rt is its last node before cap.end(); the append is a direct launch after it) so the removed cost is one graph node, which is what the harness measures. Reader audit: grep of the whole crate shows sd.kv_normed after FP:4038 is read only by the two kv_cache_append_batched launches (FP:4187 arena slot_per path, FP:4204 prefill path); the compressor reads attn_input_norm, not kv_normed. ISA: both f16_roundtrip and kv_cache_append_batched cast with the same v_cvt_f16_f32_e32 (same float mode, same build flags), so f16(f32(f16(x))) == f16(x) holds by construction, independent of the FP8-window argument. My runs (review/results/f16rt_review_run{1,2,3}.txt, label review/D_attention, quiet box): chain b=4 with/without f16rt = 8.66/6.40, 8.86/6.57, 8.81/6.54 us -> median 8.81/6.54 = 1.347x (engineer 8.6/6.4 = 1.34x); b=1: 9.70/7.23, 8.94/6.64, 8.89/6.61; b=8: 8.79/6.50, 8.84/6.55, 8.77/6.51; delta 2.26-2.47 us per lane-layer, p10/p90 tight (<1%). Correctness: engineer's 10-magnitude sweep reproduced bit-exact (0 of 2.6M cache values differ, 529K f32 values changed by the roundtrip). My extra harness (review/review_f16rt.cpp, results/review_correctness_run1.txt): 15 cases, all 0 cache-bit differences: b=1/3/5/7/8/512, n_raw_before 5/127/128, the arena slot_per scattered-slot path, and adversarial inputs the engineer did not try: exact f16 rounding ties (4096/4096 f32 values changed by the roundtrip, 0 cache bits), f16 subnormals incl. below 2^-24, |x| up to 70000 (f16 overflow -> inf both ways), -0/+-inf/NaN, and RAW un-quantized inputs (the proof does not depend on the FP8 window). Minor accounting notes (do not change the verdict): (1) est 0.18 ms/step counts 80 lane-layers of dGPU busy time; the two lanes run on separate streams, so the critical-path saving per step is closer to 40 x 2.3 = ~0.09 ms, and at <6 rows (one lane) it is ~0.09 ms; (2) the harness times fp8+f16rt+append as one graph while production has the append outside the g.kv_chain graph, but the saving is still one node either way; (3) win is a launch-floor removal (roofline 0.01 us), ~0.2% of a ~45 ms step, so it is real but small.
  - merge notes: Integration is a one-line deletion of the de.f16rt.launch block at FP:4036-4039 (stage "k.kv_chain.f16rt"). Also delete the decode twin at forward_layer.rs:909-913 for consistency (that path is the non-arena per-token decode, not the production multistream path, but it keeps the same invariant). The F16Roundtrip module/field (de.f16rt, compressor.rs:577-600) can stay or be removed once no other caller exists (grep: none besides these two). Keep the kv_cache_append (_Float16) RN cast unchanged; if anyone ever changes the append to a different rounding (RTZ/pkrtz) or adds a new f32 consumer of sd.kv_normed after FP:4038 (e.g. an f32 indexer input or a dump), the no-op argument breaks and the roundtrip must come back. Tests to run on the merge: the existing decode fidelity gate is not needed (cache bits are provably and measurably identical), but run the kv-cache bit-identity check (review/review_f16rt.cpp pattern) as a cheap regression, and confirm the evtrace stage list no longer shows k.kv_chain.f16rt. The 'kv_post_rope' subtensor dump (engine.rs:1143, forward_layer path) will now dump un-rounded f32 values; if the CPU oracle diff for that tag assumes f16-rounded values, adjust the oracle tolerance for that tag only (it is a debugging dump, not a production path).

#### `D_attention/flash_splitk` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `decode pair (score + smwsum)`
- idea: Flash-decoding split-K: grid (4 head-tiles, S = ceil(n_total/KPS), b) x 512; each WG scores its KPS keys (all 16 warps: NT key tiles x dim slices, f32 partials reduced via LDS), local softmax vs the split max (sink included), partial W.V from V fragments loaded straight into registers, (o_s, m_s, l_s) partials; a second tiny kernel combines. NOT bit-exact. Measured flash3 k128 (+combine, 3 runs): b=1/2/3/4/5/8 = 12.0/28.2/18.7/35.4/29.5/49.4 us vs 41.9/41.1/43.8/46.9/47.4/53.3 (x0.29 at b=1, x0.75 at b=4, ~x0.93 at b=8); k64: 13.3/26.0/21.2/35.3/32.4/52.9. Scales badly with b: per-WG fixed cost (q stage, 3 barriers, 32 KB of partials per WG), 1-2 WGs per WGP at 235 VGPRs, and the combine launch. DOMINATED by the bit-exact fused kernel except at b=1 (12.0 vs 19.9). Arithmetic at b=4: -11.5 us x 80 = 0.9 ms/step. Register-lean flash2 (k32: 149 VGPRs) and the first flash (cand2, spilled 28-92 VGPRs, every V load serialised behind scratch stores) are earlier iterations.
- engineer: 46.90 -> 35.40 us, x1.320, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=False; rel_rmse 2.1e-4 vs production on all shapes; vs an f64 CPU reference the flash kernels are at 1.99-2.07e-4 while production itself is at 2.13e-4 (same precision class: f16 weight rounding, here relative to the split max; f32 accumulation re-associated). Needs the fidelity gate (KLD vs golden CPU ref, pinned routing) before any merge — not run.
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=0.90, est_prefill_pct=0.00; integration_cost=medium
- files: /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention/cand4_attn.hip (attention_dec_flash3_part1_k64/k128 + attention_dec_flash3_combine), cand3_attn.hip (flash2), cand2_attn.hip (flash, spills), results/final_run{1,2,3}.txt, results/check4_run1.txt, results/att_flash2_b4/
- repro: `cd scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention && bash build2.sh all && bash final.sh final 3 attention_dec_flash3_part1_k128,attention_dec_flash3_part1_k64,attention_dec_flash2_part1_k128 && python3 agg.py results/final_run1.txt results/final_run2.txt results/final_run3.txt`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.329 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Reproduced (3 separate scheduler runs, reviewer rebuild of baselines from the unmodified in-tree attention_dec.hip / attention_mixed.hip with $KFLAGS_V41, cand3/cand4 rebuilt from the engineer's sources; results in /home/claude-code/deepstrix/.claude/worktrees/kernel-ideas-2026-09-26/scratch/kernel_ideas/D_attention/review/work/results/): flash3 k128 (+combine) vs production pair, medians of 3 medians: b=1 11.87 vs 41.73 (x0.285), b=2 28.18 vs 41.44 (x0.680), b=3 18.48 vs 43.62 (x0.424), b=4 35.30 vs 46.90 (x0.753 = 1.329x; claim 1.32), b=5 29.42 vs 47.25, b=8 49.41 vs 53.33 (x0.927). All within 1% of the engineer's numbers, p10s consistent. Baseline fidelity: harness launches the same symbols (attention_dec_score_htiled_wmma_f16s, attention_mixed_softmax_wsum_batched_htiled_wmma_ldsv_f16s), same grid (ceil(n/256),4,b)x512 and (4,b,1)x512, same 14/13 args in production order, stride 3072, max_keys_words 2568, kq_scale 1/sqrt(512), comp_kv_batch_stride 512 gathered / comp_base_per dense, graph mode with warm small operands = production decode regime (attention_dec.rs:32-77, attention.rs:1117-1150, FP:5805/5889). The 33 us smwsum vs the inventory's '~16 us' is NOT a harness artefact flattering the candidate: the '~16 us' is a parenthetical estimate in commit 98bdd94 (no measurement), while the harness's score kernel (9.7 us) matches the production paired A/B in that commit (13.6 us incl. a 4.6 us event pair). Correctness: engineer's 7-shape check reproduced identically; my extended check (10 shapes incl. nk=1 tail split, raw/comp straddle inside a split, n_total=17, no-window 0+64, b=6, b=7 ragged, b=8 max-b with tails, b=4 dense store with tails; x 4 regimes: uniform, peaked q x40, very peaked q x120 with f16 weight underflow, sink-dominated +20..30) shows no nonfinite, no tail/straddle bug, candidate error vs an f64 CPU reference over ALL rows identical to production's (2.16e-4 vs 2.22e-4 uniform; 1.9e-3 vs 1.9e-3 peaked; 2.4e-3 vs 2.4e-3 sink-dominated), candidate-vs-production rel_rmse 2e-4 (uniform) down to 2-6e-5 (peaked); worst row-level max_abs vs production 1.1e-3 on very-peaked ragged b=7/8 (16-warp f32 dim-split score re-association moving a dominant key's weight), still same vs-f64 error as production. Deviation class = benign f32 re-association of the score dot + f16 weight rounding relative to the split max instead of the global max; it is nonetheless a precision change and MUST pass the KLD-vs-golden-CPU-ref gate with pinned routing before any merge (not run, per the rules). Timing includes the combine kernel (both launches are inside the timed graph). Caveats found: (1) the combine is NOT a 'tiny kernel': measured alone 5.4 / 19.1 / 22.9 us at b=1/4/8 vs part1 alone 9.2 / 18.2 / 28.3 us (sum matches the pair-of-launches time) -- half the candidate's cost at b>=4 is the combine reading ~2.6 MB of partials, which should cost ~3 us; the engineer's diagnosis (per-WG fixed cost of part1) is mostly mis-attributed. (2) non-monotonic b=2 (28.2) > b=3 (18.5) reproduced, unexplained WG-placement effect; at the production-typical b=2 the win is only x0.68. (3) The engineer's own bit-exact fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4 (20.3 us flat at b=1..8, separately CONFIRMED in WINS.log) dominates flash3 everywhere except b=1 (11.9 vs 19.9), as the claim itself states. (4) dGPU pm sysfs returns EBUSY (cannot read sclk); DPM auto, unpinned; interleaved A/B so the ratio is fair, absolute numbers may move with clocks. (5) est 0.9 ms/step = 80 lane-layer dGPU busy time at b=4; per-lane critical path ~0.46 ms (~0.15% of a 300 ms step); L0-1 have n_total=128 (S=1) so slightly less. No skipped work: all b*64*512 outputs written, poisoned partials would have produced NaN and did not. No GPU processes left running.
  - merge notes: Do not integrate flash3 as-is: it is dominated by the bit-exact fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4 at every b except 1 and is not bit-exact. If revisited: (a) fix the combine first (fold into part1 via a last-arriving-WG atomic ticket, or a vectorized combine; target ~3 us at b=4), which would bring flash3 to ~21 us at b=4 (still only at parity with the bit-exact kernel); (b) then run the project's fidelity gate (KLD vs golden CPU reference, pinned routing, real model activations; the peaked-softmax regime is where it deviates most from production at the row level). - repro: `none`

**Dead ends (D_attention):** (1) fused_direct (B fragments gathered straight from global, 16 u16 loads per tile per lane): 42-43 us, no gain — the CU memory pipeline drains 2-line u16 gathers at ~3.7 cycles per wave-instruction (ATT), so phase B is load-INSTRUCTION-bound; its GROUP=32 variants spill and miscompute on partial tiles (not debugged). (2) fused_qlds (q staged as f16 in LDS): 37.5 us, slower than fused_f16s (33.2): the score phase is per-warp latency-bound, the LDS q adds a round trip + barrier; casting q once into 128 VGPRs (qreg) is the right form. (3) Deeper V prefetch in the LDS-V phase B (pf2r4, pf1r8): identical to pf2r2 — not load-latency-bound. (4) Warp-private V ring in the natural layout (wpriv, no WG barriers): 36-38 us, no gain — not barrier-bound; the 2-tile interleaved score phase (s2) spilled (51 us). What finally worked was cutting LDS instructions: transposed V tile (2 x b64 per fragment) + tile-major weights (1 x b128): 27.3 us; + qreg: 20.3; + software pipelining: 19.9-21.8. (5) Flash split-K (three iterations: cand2 spilled 28-92 VGPRs with every V load serialised behind scratch stores, 23-27 us at b=1 / 66-79 at b=4; flash2 register-lean 13.5 at b=1 / 40 at b=4; flash3 16-warp score phase 12.0 at b=1 / 35.4 at b=4): wins only at b=1, scales badly with b (per-WG fixed cost: q stage, 3 barriers, 32 KB partials per WG; 1-2 WGs per WGP; combine launch) and is not bit-exact — dominated by the bit-exact fused kernel at every b >= 2. (6) ATT on 4-WG kernels at b=1 yields no rows (CU 1 gets no WG): profile at b=8. (7) Attempt-1's score comparisons were invalid (compared against the weights-overwritten scores buffer); fixed in attn_harness2.cpp with a pristine copy.

**Notes (D_attention):** Baseline = the unmodified in-tree attention_dec.hip / attention_mixed.hip compiled with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco for gfx1201, launched with the production grid/block/args (scores stride 3072, max_keys_words 2568, gathered comp store stride 512 or dense store with comp_base_per, mask null), synthetic warm operands, kb::ab graph mode (10 calls per graph), quiet box (hub down, both GPUs idle) — see NOTES.md for the full log. Discrepancy to flag: the production smwsum measures 33 us per call here at 640 keys, not the ~16 us the inventory quotes (commit 98bdd94 bench); the harness figures are self-consistent across ~20 runs and the ATT trace shows 40 tile iterations of 2 dependent global round trips each, so I stand by 33 us for this shape — an integration pass should re-time the production pair in-situ (bench_decode_latency_ab) before crediting the 2.0 ms/step estimate. All estimates use 80 lane-layers per step (2 lanes x 40 layers) and per-lane-layer deltas at b=3-4; the two lanes overlap on the dGPU, so the per-lane critical-path saving is ~1.0 ms and the dGPU-busy saving ~2.0 ms. The bit-exact fused kernel (cand7 attention_dec_fused_vt_qreg_sp_d4) replaces the two launches at FP:5805/5889 whenever attn_dec_score_for(b) (b<=16) and n_total_max <= 640; it needs the same wrapper plumbing as attention_dec.rs (out, sinks, q, raw_kv, comp_kv, n_raw_per, n_raw_offset_per, n_comp_per, n_head, kq_scale, comp_kv_batch_stride, comp_base_per), grid (n_head/16, b) x 512, 62 KB static LDS, and must fall back to the pair when a comp_allowed_bits mask is ever passed (decode passes none). Its numerics are the production chain instruction for instruction (same per-tile WMMA order, same f16 rounding points, same phase-A shuffle tree), verified bit-exact on 7 shapes including tails, dense store, empty rows and b=8. No cargo, no in-tree edits, no GPU work outside the scheduler; no GPU jobs left running. Prefill attention was not touched (est_prefill_pct = 0 everywhere); the decode-only fused kernel does not apply above 640 keys.

#### `B_moe_prefill/wmma_i8_gateup` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_kwide -> mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_wmma (cand_wmma.hip)`
- idea: int8 WMMA arm: v_wmma_i32_16x16x16_iu8 with A = Q8_K activations (16 members x 16 k, staged once per WG+super-block in LDS, 32 members = 1-2 M tiles) and B = MXFP4 weights unpacked per lane with v_perm to w' = 12 + kvalue (unsigned 0..24); the accumulator is pre-loaded with -12*sum32(a) from the Q8_K bsums the production quantizer already writes, so the int32 result is exactly the production integer dot; per 32-block acc += (2^(e-128)*yd)*(float)C. 128 rows per WG (8 waves x 16 rows), grid (n_rows/128, n_work_items), same argument list as kwide, honours n_work_items_dev. VALU instructions -51%, SALU -98%, LDS -91% vs kwide (PMC). Leverage: documented 39.1 ms/layer at B=1024 x 0.609 = 23.8 ms -> -15.3 ms/layer = 40 layers/1024 rows -> -0.60 ms per prefill token of iGPU kernel time; box-2 MoE GPU ~52% of the 100K wall, gate+up = 55% of the MoE chain -> ~ -11% prefill wall if box 2 stays the critical path.
- engineer: 12035.00 -> 7269.00 us, x1.640, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=False; identical integer sums; f32 accumulation order differs (per-block scaled int32 sums vs kwide's 16-element lane partials + 32-lane tree): rel_rmse 5.4e-7, max_abs 3.8e-4, 11% of elements differ in bits, no non-finite; checked at B = 37/128/512/1024/2048 (2048 = >32 members -> chunked work items) with the bound grid and the exact grid; needs the fidelity gate (KLD vs golden CPU ref, pinned routing) because mid goes through q8_k_quantize
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=-0.60, est_prefill_pct=-11.00; integration_cost=medium
