- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x1.630 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: CONFIRMED, but the headline magnitude is overstated by ~5% because of the harness's 96-of-384 expert regime. (1) Baseline fidelity: rebuilt all six baseline hsacos and the candidate from the unmodified in-tree sources with $KFLAGS_V41 in review/gateup/: md5-identical to the engineer's (candidate disassembly identical, md5 differs only by source path). Harness launches the production symbol mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_kwide with the same 17 args/types as mxfp4_pair.rs launch_fused_swiglu_kwide_ex, grid (2304/8, moe_wi_upper_bound)x256, chunk 32, clamp 10.0 (SWIGLU_CLAMP_EXP), n_blocks 20, device-count bound (V41_MOE_WI_DEVCOUNT default on, forward_prefill.rs:8821 / remote_experts.rs:4421), cold weights (1.2 GB/launch >> 32 MB MALL), warm Q8_K activations from the production q8_k_quantize (which writes the bsums the candidate needs; box 2 receives full 5840-B Q8_K rows so bsums are present there too), direct launch (prefill is not graph-captured). (2) Repro: engineer's exact command 3x via the scheduler: B=1024 gate+up ratios 0.618/0.610/0.612 -> median 1.63x (claimed 1.64); B=512 0.802/0.808/0.827 (claimed 0.810); B=128 0.974-0.977 (neutral, as claimed). (3) Correctness: not bit-exact, benign f32 re-association (identical int32 sums; per-block scaled 32-elem sums in one chain vs kwide's 16-elem lane partials + 32-lane tree). rel_rmse 5.4e-7 / max_abs <5e-4 on O(10) values at B=1,7,33,37,128,512,1023,1024 (2 seeds, n_exp=8 -> chunked), 2048, 4096 (max_group 86 = 3 work items/expert), bound and exact grids, no non-finite, 96-sample CPU spot check of the baseline passes. CAVEAT on the engineer's cmp inputs: with x in [-1,1] and E8M0 scales up to 2^0 the dots have std ~77, so ~90% of gate/up outputs hit the +-10 clamp and are bit-identical regardless of numerics (that is exactly the '11% differ' figure). In the realistic 2^-10..2^-4 regime (KB_SC_LO=118 KB_SC_SPAN=7) 23% differ, rel_rmse 3.2e-7, max_abs 5.7e-5 on O(4) values; no-clamp regime rel_rmse 3.7e-7 -> still benign, but mid feeds q8_k_quantize so the KLD fidelity gate (golden CPU ref, pinned routing) is required before merge, as the engineer states. (4) Traps: no skipped work (same outputs, row_valid/mi<n_members guards, chunk clamp min(chunk_size,32) matches the wrapper's assert), no extra kernel needed, same flags. The one real distortion: with 96 physical experts, 480 of 576 grid.y rows are empty tail work-groups; kwide pays that tail over 288 grid.x WGs, the candidate over 18. Exact-grid A/B at 96 experts: 0.634 (B=1024) / 0.839 (B=512) vs bound 0.625 / 0.818 in the same job. Full-layer A/B with all 384 experts physically present (7.2 GB, production bound grid, 3 runs): B=1024 kwide 45.8/46.0/46.9 ms -> cand 29.6/29.5/29.8 ms, ratio 0.646/0.642/0.635 = 1.56x; B=512 31.8/31.7/31.7 -> 27.0/27.0/26.4, ratio 0.849/0.850/0.831 = 1.18x. So the true production ratio is ~0.64 not 0.61 at B=1024 and ~0.85 not 0.81 at B=512. Leverage arithmetic: -16.4 ms/layer measured at B=1024 (DPM auto; engineer's -15.3 from the documented 39.1 ms is consistent in ratio terms) -> ~0.64 ms/token of iGPU time at 1024-row passes, but only ~0.40 ms/token (5.1 ms/layer) at the 512-row passes box 1 runs and box 2 often runs; the '-11% prefill wall' is an upper bound that becomes ~-9% (1024-row passes) / ~-4% (512-row passes) even if box 2 stays the critical path. Candidate uses 128-bit global loads at 8-byte-aligned addresses (odd super-blocks) and 4-byte-aligned Q8_K qs; correct on gfx11 (unaligned mode) and confirmed by every cmp, but the production header explicitly warns wider-than-8-byte loads on this layout; the candidate's own loads-only ablation reaching 180 GB/s says it is fine here. Note: another reviewer concurrently overwrote build.sh/run_extra.sh in review/ (shared dir); my work is isolated in review/gateup/ (results/, harness_gu.cpp with KB_SC_LO/KB_SC_SPAN/KB_EXACT_GRID knobs, run_*.sh); one of my early tickets (000312) ran their script instead of mine, harmless.
  - merge notes: Integration = new kernel symbol mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_wmma in K/mxfp4_pair_matvec.hip (or a sibling file) with the SAME argument list as kwide, plus: (a) wrapper launch_fused_swiglu_wmma_ex in S/mxfp4_pair.rs with grid (ceil(n_rows/128), n_work_items, 1) x 256 (kwide uses n_rows/8) and asserts n_rows%128==0 is NOT required (row_valid guard) but chunk_size<=32 and n_blocks>0 are; the kernel silently clamps chunk to 32 so keep the wrapper assert; (b) dispatch.rs moe_gate_up_chunked_ex MXFP4 arm gated by a knob (e.g. V41_MXFP4_PAIR_VARIANT=wmma) so kwide stays the rollback; (c) it honours n_work_items_dev exactly like kwide, so moe_wi_devcount_supported can include it; (d) production xq must carry bsums: true for q8_k_quantize on both GPUs and for the Q8_K rows shipped to box 2 (XQ_BYTES_PER_TOKEN=5840), but any future Q8_K producer (CPU oracle path, f16 cast path) must write bsums or the kernel is silently wrong -> add a test that zeroes bsums and expects failure, or compute -12*sum from qs in-kernel as a fallback; (e) NOT bit-exact -> run the KLD fidelity gate (golden CPU ref, pinned routing) at prefill B=512 and B=1024 before enabling; (f) expect 1.56x at 1024-row passes and 1.18x at 512-row passes (box-1 lane shape is 512), neutral at B<=128, so decode (family A, b<=8) should NOT switch; (g) 230 VGPR / 6 waves per SIMD and 10.1 KB LDS: re-measure inside the shipped multi-kernel module and alongside the concurrent decode lanes on the hub; (h) test tails: B odd (1023 ok), >32 members per expert (2048/4096 ok), n_exp small (ok); (i) re-run the engineer's kwide vs wmma A/B once on the real expert pool layout (gate/up base pointers into pool slots: the kernel only needs 8-byte alignment of super-blocks, which the 136-B stride guarantees); (j) the down kernel (mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_wmma) is a separate claim reviewed separately; my full-layer numbers for it: 0.369/0.376/0.368 at B=1024 (2.7x), 0.543/0.560/0.544 at B=512.

#### `B_moe_prefill/wmma_i8_down` — measured_win — review: CONFIRMED
- kernel: `mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_kwide2 -> mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_wmma (cand_wmma.hip)`
- idea: Same int8-WMMA design for the down projection (one matrix, n_blocks_in = 9, xq indexed by (b*n_used+slot)*xq_slot_stride, writes the same [B*n_used, n_rows] partials so q2_k_reduce_partials_hetsplit is unchanged). Kills kwide2's per-row-pair global re-reads of the activations (13x the weight bytes) by staging each member's super-block once per WG in LDS and sharing it across 128 rows. 133 VGPR / 11 waves per SIMD; 185-209 GB/s = 80-90% of the 230 GB/s streaming roof (loads-only ablation of the same kernel: 207 GB/s, so it is at its access-pattern floor). Leverage: 31.8 ms/layer x 0.356 = 11.3 ms -> -20.5 ms/layer -> -0.80 ms per prefill token of iGPU kernel time; down = 45% of the MoE chain -> ~ -15% prefill wall. Both WMMA kernels together: MoE chain 70.9 -> 35.1 ms/layer at B=1024 = 2.02x, ~ -24% of the 100K prefill wall as an upper bound.
- engineer: 9640.00 -> 3374.00 us, x2.810, runs=3
- correctness: bit_exact=False; identical integer sums, f32 order differs: rel_rmse 1.7e-7, max_abs 6.8e-3 on O(1e2) values, 21% of elements differ in bits, no non-finite; B = 37/128/512/1024/2048, bound + exact grid; fidelity gate needed
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=-0.80, est_prefill_pct=-15.00; integration_cost=medium
- files: cand_wmma.hip (mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_wmma), harness.cpp (l_down_wmma), results/wmma_ab_B*_run{1,2}.txt, results/cand_wmma_ab_B*_run3.txt, results/wmma_cmp_B*_run1.txt, results/tmp_wNC_ab_B1024_run1.txt (loads-only floor)
- repro: `same job as wmma_i8_gateup (run_cand.sh cand_wmma 9 all 128 1024 512 128); B=512 0.524 [0.516..0.535] = 1.91x, B=128 0.767 = 1.30x`
- REVIEW (CONFIRMED): reran x2.800 runs=3 correctness_ok=True baseline_matches_production=True
  - issues: Kernel-level claim reproduces and the baseline is faithful. Baseline: my base_mxfp4_matvec_gfx1151.hsaco built from the unmodified in-tree kernels/mxfp4_matvec.hip (git diff 361d4f9..HEAD on crates/ is empty) with $KFLAGS_V41 --genco is byte-identical (md5 74d50cdf...) to the engineer's; the harness launches mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_kwide2 with grid (5120/16, n_wi_bound=576 at B=1024) x 256 and the exact argument list of src/mxfp4.rs:147 launch_by_expert_kwide2_ex as called from forward_prefill.rs:9059 (dbpe, xq_slot_stride=9*292, n_used=6, max_per_expert, chunk 32, n_rows 5120, n_blocks_in 9, device work-item count pointer). Regime: 96 expert copies = 0.60 GB down weights per launch through a 32 MB MALL (cold), midq warm as in production (written by q8_k_quantize just before), direct launch (prefill is not graph-captured; V41_MS_GRAPHS is the decode path). Rerun (review/results/ab_B*_run101-103, seeds 101-103, 20 rounds x 2, interleaved): B=1024 prod 9584/9597/9736 us vs cand 3426/3463/3444 us = ratios 0.3575/0.3609/0.3538 -> median 2.80x (claim 2.81x); B=512 0.523/0.514/0.513 (claim 1.91x); B=128 0.78/0.77/0.75. Extra: full 384-expert layer (7.2 GB weights, results/ab_full384_*): B=1024 36.42 -> 13.56 ms (0.372, 2.69x), B=512 21.33 -> 11.78 ms (0.552) -- the x4 scaling from 96 experts is ~4% optimistic. Correctness: not bit-exact, rel_rmse 1.66e-7 at every shape (B=1, 3, 37, 128, 512, 1024, 2048 with chunked >32-member groups), no non-finite; a DC-offset activation set (mid in [0.5,1], all-positive q8 codes, large bsums) that stresses the -12*bsum offset trick gives rel_rmse 5e-10; row-count tails n_rows=16/5008/5104/5120 with sentinel-filled partials show the candidate writes exactly the same (b,slot,row) set as kwide2 (matters: the hub path does NOT zero partials, FP 'No partials zero-fill (M53)'). Deviation class: identical int32 dot products, f32 re-association only (per-block scale*yd*C chain vs kwide2's per-lane partial + 32-lane tree); kwide2 itself is 2.4e-6 rel from the CPU reference, so this is benign but still needs the project's KLD gate. Reviewer/engineer disassembly of mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_wmma is identical (133 VGPR, 0 scratch) even though the engineer's cand_wmma.hip (19:49) postdates their hsaco (19:45). Leverage arithmetic is overstated: est_ms_per_token=-0.80 assumes 40 layers x 1024 rows, but CED prefill runs only the 20 encoder layers at full batch (L20-39 are replay on <=128 rows, where the down win is ~1.3x); the honest figure is ~-0.4 ms/token summed over both boxes (~-0.2 ms/token on the box-2 critical path), and est_prefill_pct=-15 is a compute-only upper bound that will not appear in the wall while prefill is box-2 NVMe-bound (memory 09-24: GPUs 17-25% busy, chunk compute 89% waiting on box 2). Housekeeping: review/ turned out to be shared with the gate+up reviewer; my Write of review/build.sh and review/run_extra.sh overwrote files that already existed there (their gateup/ subdir, run_realistic.sh, harness.cpp copy are untouched). Candidate relies on 16-byte global loads at 8-byte/4-byte aligned addresses (136-B super-blocks, 292-B Q8_K rows); this is correct under ROCm's unaligned-access mode and verified by the cmp, but the production code deliberately used 8-byte loads there.
  - merge notes: Integration: add mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_wmma (cand_wmma.hip, template wm_body<1,1>) to kernels/mxfp4_matvec.hip or a new .hip in build.rs; new wrapper in src/mxfp4.rs with grid (ceil(n_rows/128), n_work_items) x 256 and the identical argument list/semantics of launch_by_expert_kwide2_ex (keep the chunk_size<=32 check: the kernel silently clamps to WM_MAX_CHUNK=32 and would drop members above it; keep the n_rows%16 or relax it, the kernel handles any n_rows); switch forward_prefill.rs:9059 and remote_experts.rs:4484 (expertd, box 2 runs the same kernel at 512-1024 rows) behind an env knob with kwide2 as rollback; q2_k_reduce_partials_hetsplit, builders and the no-zero-fill partials contract are unchanged. Requires: the Q8_K producer must write bsums (production q8_k_quantize does, including the dGPU-side xq for box 2); a fidelity gate (KLD vs golden CPU ref, pinned routing) since it is f32-reassociated not bit-exact; re-measure on both boxes under real routing and with the hub live (my numbers are hub-down, DPM auto); check decode b>=2 on box 2 (B=128-like regime gives ~1.3x, family A territory) and that 10.1 KB LDS + 133 VGPR co-schedule with the gate+up kernel. Re-state the leverage as ~-0.4 ms/token of summed iGPU kernel time (20 encoder layers), wall gain conditional on prefill leaving the disk-bound regime (layer-major prefill).

#### `B_moe_prefill/wmma_i8_x16_exchange` — blocked
- kernel: `mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_wmma (cand_wmma4.hip)`
- idea: Lanes 0-15 load/unpack blocks 0-3 and lanes 16-31 blocks 4-7 of the same row (17 VGPRs per matrix instead of 34, no address loaded twice by the two wave halves), complete each block's B fragment in all 32 lanes with v_permlanex16 (half swap) + v_cndmask (-31% unpack VALU), spend the freed registers on a 2-buffer prefetch. #### `B_moe_prefill/fused_down_reduce` — untested
- kernel: `mxfp4_matvec_par_by_expert_wmma + q2_k_reduce_partials_hetsplit`
- idea: Accumulate the down projection directly into out[b][row] (f32 atomicAdd over the 6 slots) instead of writing [B x 6 x 5120] partials and reducing: saves the 0.82 ms reduce + 126 MB partials write/read per 1024 rows (~7% of the new down+reduce time). Float atomics make the sum order non-deterministic, which the project's determinism rule for expert-cache scoring forbids; a deterministic fusion would need a fixed slot order = the reduce we already have.
- engineer: 821.00 -> 0.00 us, x0.000, runs=0
- correctness: bit_exact=False; not run; would not be bit-exact (non-deterministic f32 sum order)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=-0.03, est_prefill_pct=-0.50; integration_cost=medium
- files: NOTES.md (IDEAS 6)

#### `B_moe_prefill/kwide_next_sb_prefetch` — untested
- kernel: `mxfp4_pair_matvec_fused_swiglu_kwide (production)`
- idea: Cheap knob on the production kernel: issue the next super-block pair's 8-B weight loads before the dot phase (double-buffer 10 VGPRs) to overlap the vmcnt(3) wait the ATT trace shows at the top of each iteration. Bounded by the ablation: removing ALL weight loads only saves 13% at B=1024 (0.865x) and 26% at B=512 — superseded by the WMMA arm.
- engineer: 12092.00 -> 0.00 us, x0.000, runs=0
- correctness: bit_exact=True; not run (would be bit-exact: same arithmetic)
- leverage: est_ms_per_token=-0.10, est_prefill_pct=-2.00; integration_cost=small
- files: cand_ablate.hip (kw_abl_noload / nodot / nostage / nolut / noload_nodot), results/ablate_B{1024,512}_run{1,2}.txt
- repro: `bash ../_infra/gpu_run.sh --dev igpu --mb 2100 --label B_moe_prefill/ablate -- bash run_ablate.sh 9`

**Dead ends (B_moe_prefill — routed MoE at prefill batch (MXFP4 gate+up kwide, down kwide2, q8_k_quantize, builders, reduce) on the iGPU gfx1151):** (1) Software-pipelined weight loads in the WMMA kernels: the gate/up-alternating version (cand_wmma2) spills (256 VGPR + 534-886 spills) and is 2.1-3.1x slower than production; the split-matrix-wave version (cand_wmma3, 159 VGPR, 9 waves/SIMD) is correct but ~4% slower than the plain cand_wmma; the matrix-sequential pipelined flavour (cand_wmma5 -DWM5_PIPE) is neutral; the pipelined down kernel is neutral (0.361x vs 0.356x). Loads-only vs full ablation shows why: the WMMA gate+up already sits within 7% of its own access-pattern floor (6.8 ms loads-only vs 7.3 ms full), so latency hiding has nothing left to buy. (2) Half-row split loads + v_permlanex16 exchange (cand_wmma4): spills, and the split access pattern itself is 1.23x slower in a loads-only test (147 vs 180 GB/s) — fewer, wider-spread load instructions keep fewer requests in flight. (3) Non-temporal (slc) weight loads: 1.4x slower on both kernels. (4) A first 'nostage' ablation of kwide read an impossible 391 GB/s: LLVM folds loads of never-written LDS to undef and DCEs the whole weight path — any ablation must keep LDS defined (stage once). (5) The 'compute roofline' worry in PREFILL_100K_PROFILE is settled: kwide is issue/latency-bound in its per-member loop (removing all weight traffic saves only 13%; VALU ~50% busy, SALU 42% as many instructions as VALU, v_movrels lane_pacc indexing), not DRAM-bound and not at a hard VALU ceiling — the int8 WMMA arm cuts VALU 2x, SALU 50x, LDS 10x and makes both kernels bandwidth-bound at 170-209 GB/s. (6) rocprofv3 on gfx1151: GRBM_GUI_ACTIVE reads a constant (broken); SQ_INST_CYCLES_*, SQ_WAIT_INST_ANY, VALUBusy, MemUnitStalled do not exist; SQ_INSTS_VALU/SALU/LDS/WAVES, SQ_BUSY_CYCLES, SQ_WAVE_CYCLES, MemUnitBusy work.
